# Bastions through `via:` (P10 S5, D23, OP2, OP7, OP8, PRD-3) Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking. This plan is executed by the wave runner of `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-07-review-remediation.md` ("Reusable workflow skeleton"): lane agents work in git worktrees, never commit, and hand back ownership-filtered patches; the integrator commits once per lane. Where a task says "Hand back", that is the lane's last step, not a commit.

**Commits at:** `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-10-p10-s5-bastions.md`

**Goal:** Build slice S5 of the approved access-model design (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-08-access-model-fleet-reach-design.md`, section "S5. Bastions through `via:` (D23; PRD-3)", plus its Shared rules and the S5 row of "Order and parallel lanes"): a per-server `via:` that reaches a server through a bastion with strict `known_hosts` on every hop, `jump_only: true` for a bastion SSHepherd tunnels through but never manages (OP8), one entry and one route per address (OP7), a fixed cap of 8 connections per bastion (OP2), dependents-before-bastions write order, the forwarding guard and post-write forward probe, `via_failed` and hop-aware host-key alerts, and `import` mapping `ProxyJump` chains onto `via:`.

**Architecture:** `internal/config` owns the schema and every reach rule (`via`, `jump_only`, `Chain`, `Dependents`; a `jump_only` server is skipped by `*`, `@tag`, the operator's implicit grant and `Covers`). `sshconn.ConnectWith` stays the one choke point: a new `dialTarget` returns either a TCP socket or a `direct-tcpip` channel through a fresh hop connection (one per target, never pooled), the hop is closed with the target, and the hop's socket carries the target's deadlines. The domain packages see a bastion only through the stdlib leaf `internal/fleet` (`HopLimiter`, `FailedHopOf`, `WriteRequest.ProbeForward`), so `make layering` stays green. `audit.Run` and `apply.Execute` take bastion slots before the per-server budget starts; `apply.Execute` writes in levels, dependents first.

**Tech Stack:** Go 1.27.3 (`GOTOOLCHAIN=go1.27.3`, the Makefile's `GO_PIN`), `golang.org/x/crypto/ssh` (`Client.DialContext` = `direct-tcpip`, `OpenChannelError`/`Prohibited`) and `ssh/knownhosts`, `gopkg.in/yaml.v3`, Cobra, the hermetic `internal/sshconn/sshtest` server (gains `direct-tcpip`), goleak, golden files (`-update`), native fuzzing (`FuzzLoad`, new `FuzzParseJump`), golangci-lint (depguard `domain`/`transport`/`fleet-leaf`, gocyclo 15, revive), `deadcode ./cmd/sshepherd`, `make layering`, the dockerized sshd matrix (`make integration`, alpine/debian/rocky) with a third, internal-only container.

**Base:** `master` at `51e7ea0`, after S1, S2, S8, S3a, S3b and S4. Every `file:line` below was read at `ce621d9`; `51e7ea0` adds only `internal/apply/protected_dedupe_test.go` (inside lane `writepath`'s `internal/apply/*`), so no cited line moved. P08 and P09 have landed: the design's "until P09-impl lands" notes no longer apply, the hop offers keys under the landed D17 rules (`Dialer.PreferKeys` = `audit.GrantedFingerprints`, granted keys only, falling back to every held key with a warning), and a probe that "could not check" is P08's `OutcomeUnproven` (`fleet.UnprovenError`), not `failed`.

**D28 for this slice.** Exit 2 gains exactly one input: a changed, changed-type or `@revoked` host key on a bastion during `audit` (it already paged for the server itself). `apply`, `revoke`, `rotate` and `adopt` exit 1 for the same bastion fault, in phase 1 and at the post-write login check alike. A `jump_only` server never changes an exit code; `jump_only_unused` is a warning (exit 0, `validate --strict` 1); a refused forward is 1; every new load error is 3. JSON stays schema 1: `via`, `via_failed`, `status: "jump_only"`, `class: "jump_only"`, `summary.jump_only`, `sweep.jump_only` and the `jump_only_unused` warning kind are additive and omitted when empty, so every existing golden holds except the `DialHint` mirrors the design names.

**Compile status.** The step that wrote this plan could write only this plan file, so no Go block below was compiled or run. Every complete file and function was checked by reading against `51e7ea0`, and every helper a block calls was read there; fragments (struct fields, `switch` cases, mid-function lines) were checked by reading. The lane that applies a block compiles it; on a compile error it fixes the block within its stated intent and reports the fix. Goldens and corpus entries named below do not exist yet; the lane generates them (`-update`) and reads each one back before accepting it.

## Design gaps and contradictions (for the main session)

No item overrides an answered decision (D23, OP2, OP7, OP8 are final). Items 1-3 are real holes in the design; the rest are readings of text the design leaves open. Task 30 Step 2 records the ones that change design text. The main session can veto any of them. **One item needs the main session's confirmation before the wave runs: item 17** (the OP7 wording); no item needs a new owner answer.

1. **`SessionTimeout` must move the hop's socket deadline too.** The design's fact 1 says only the hop's raw socket enforces a tunnelled connection's budget, and the hop is dialed with the target's `ctx`. But `ConnectOptions.SessionTimeout` (the login probe's `probeExecDeadline`, `internal/sshwrite/probe.go:116`) is applied after the target's handshake with `conn.SetDeadline` (`internal/sshconn/connect.go:238-240`), which a `direct-tcpip` channel ignores. Left alone, a probe through a bastion would keep the dial bound as its socket deadline, die mid-exec, read as `stageAuthed`, and **restore a good file** (`verdictRejected`). The plan gives `Conn` its raw transport and its hop, and `setDeadline` moves both (Task 9).
2. **`via_failed` names the bastion only when the bastion is at fault.** The design says `HopStage` is "set on any failure reaching the hop or opening the forward". A bastion that opens the channel but cannot reach the target answers `ConnectionFailed`, which is the dependent's own failure (it is down, or firewalled from the bastion). Marking it `via_failed` would send the operator to the wrong machine and would count it toward hop host-key dedupe. The plan sets `HopStage` for every failure connecting to the hop (dial, host key, auth, handshake, timeout) and for a `Prohibited` forward (kind `dial`, the forward hint), and gives a `ConnectionFailed`/other rejection kind `dial` with no `HopStage` and a new `HopDialHint` (`the bastion could not reach this server; check that it is up and reachable from the bastion`). `DialHint`'s second clause ("a host reachable only through a bastion needs via: in the manifest") would be wrong advice for a server that already has `via:`. In the login probe both read as "could not check" (`verdictTransient`, then UNPROVEN), as the design wants.
3. **The forwarding guard is "at least one survivor" (design text), but sshd uses the first key that logs in.** The hop offers the bastion's granted keys in `authkeys.OfferOrder`; if the first held one sits on a `no-port-forwarding` line, the hop logs in with it and forwarding fails although another survivor could forward. The post-write forward probe closes this: it runs on the login probe's connection, which offers the survivors in the same order (`probeOrderFor`), so that case is a `Prohibited` forward and a restore. The plan implements the design's static rule and relies on the probe for the order case; Task 22's test pins both.
4. **The guard does not re-run after the prompt.** `applyOne` re-checks only `Survivors` against the fresh listing (`internal/apply/exec.go:106`). A forwarding key that vanished during the prompt is caught by the forward probe (restore), not refused up front. No code; recorded.
5. **The via hint's quoting and a deeper chain.** The design shows only a one-hop `ssh -p 22 jump@bastion.example.com ssh-keyscan …` form. The plan prints a word bare when it matches `^[A-Za-z0-9._:@-]+$` and, otherwise, quoted twice (once for the local shell, once for the bastion's) so no character in a manifest host reaches a shell live; outer hops of a deeper chain go in one `-J user@host:port,…`. The label is the design's verbatim: `(as seen by the bastion; still compare with the console)`. The direct (no `via`) form is byte-identical to today.
6. **Report text the design leaves open.** Audit's row: `bastion-eu (jump@bastion.example.com:22)  jump only (not audited: nothing reads its authorized_keys)`. Apply's row: `… jump only (never read or written)`. Both summaries add ` · N jump only` only when N > 0 (`Summary: 1/2 servers compliant · … · 1 jump only  → exit 0`; the denominator includes it, as `summary.servers` does). A via server's head gains ` via NAME` inside the parentheses (`web-1 (deploy@10.0.1.5:22 via bastion-eu)`), so reports with no `via:` stay byte-identical. `logFleetReadDone` adds `, J jump only` when J > 0. `--sweep` prints `not swept: bastion-eu (jump only)` (design text) and JSON gains `sweep.jump_only` (additive, omitted when empty; the design names no field).
7. **`exitCodesWrite` changes.** Its exit-2 line says a changed host key "at the post-write login check" pages; after S5 that is the server's own key only (design: "exit 2 for audit only"). The help text becomes `… at the post-write login check (the server's own; a bastion's is 1) …`, so `cmd/sshepherd/testdata/help/{apply,revoke,rotate}.golden` and the README's write-command exit footers change (Task 26).
8. **`needsHuman` excludes a hop's host-key alarm.** `fleet.UnprovenError.Unwrap` keeps the last probe error, so `fleet.FailedHopOf` sees the hop; an UNPROVEN probe stopped by a bastion's host-key alarm is exit 1, the server's own is still 2 (D10b).
9. **`manifest/edit.go` needs no code.** The design lists it because `RemoveAccess`'s fleet list must leave `jump_only` out. `selectorReach` (`internal/manifest/edit.go:373-385`) asks `config.SelectorMatches`, which now answers false for a `jump_only` server, so a `*` entry left covering only `jump_only` servers is deleted with no edit. Task 5 pins it with a test in lane `schema`.
10. **One route per address also binds `import`.** The loader refuses two entries at one `host:port` behind different hops, even with different users (design Validation). The design's import text covers only a repeated **target**. Without more, `import` of `web` (direct) and `app` (same IP, another user, `ProxyJump bastion`) would fail its own self-check as a "bug:" (exit 3). The plan drops the later host the same way, with a second message: `server "app" in the ssh config is at the same address as "web" but takes a different route — skipped, and its access is not moved; every entry at one address must use the same route` (Task 18).
11. **Import's route model.** A host's own route counts: a direct host needs parent `""`, an unmapped proxy needs `?`, a manifest server needs its manifest `via`. A chain `a,b` needs `b`'s parent to be `a` and the target's to be `b`; its first hop keeps its own route (OpenSSH connects a `-J` hop with that hop's own config), so `ProxyJump inner` where `inner` is itself behind `bastion` maps to a two-hop chain. A hop resolves by alias, or by host and port (22 when unset) and user (any when unset) to exactly one server; an explicit `user@` or `:port` must match. `ssh://` URIs and `ProxyCommand` are not mapped. A chain whose hop's own proxy is unmapped, that loops, or that ends deeper than 3 is not mapped. Same-target comparison uses the resolved chain; an unmapped proxy compares by its raw text.
12. **Import notes for `jump_only`.** "Notes it" is read as an info-level `plan.Notes` line, once per server, from both `knownKey` (extension) and `newUser` (grant); the fleet set behind `coversFleet` leaves `jump_only` out, so `*` compaction still happens.
13. **`scripts/integration.sh` belongs to lane `transport`, not the integrator.** Round 2's real-sshd tests need the bastion under `make integration`. Container A becomes the bastion (connected to a new `--internal` network), a third container C sits on that network only and is addressed by its IP there: a container started on the default bridge does not reliably get Docker's embedded DNS after `docker network connect`. "DNS is resolved by the bastion" is proven hermetically instead: the test hop receives the unresolved hostname `localhost` (Task 9).
14. **Round 1 lands `via:` in the schema before the transport honours it.** Between round 1's and round 2's commits, a manifest with `via:` loads but its server would be dialed directly. No release can be cut between them (releases are tagged, gated, and the wave commits rounds back to back), and the README documents `via:` only from round 2's commit. Recorded so a verifier does not flag it.
15. **`FuzzLoad`'s committed corpus is already at its 16-entry cap.** S5 adds an in-code `f.Add` seed and extends the oracle, with no committed entry. The new `FuzzParseJump` ships its own committed corpus (6 entries), so `make fuzz-list` counts one more target than at `51e7ea0` (Task 30 checks the count against the runner's own `make fuzz-list` output, not a hard-coded number).
16. **The `DialHint` mirrors.** Lane `transport` (round 2) owns the new `DialHint`, `internal/audit/report_test.go`'s and `internal/apply/report_test.go`'s `testDialHint`, and the goldens that carry it, because `TestAuditGoldenCarriesDialHint` (`internal/sshconn/connect_test.go:353`) reads them in the same commit. Lane `writepath` (round 3) owns `internal/apply` after that commit.
17. **OP7 wording — main session confirms before the wave runs.** The recorded answer (design line 101) says "`target` stays host, port and user; a repeat is a load error"; the design's Validation text says "`target` does not change (host, port, user and file path)". `target` today is host, port, user and effective path (`internal/config/config.go:270-275`), so "stays" reads as "unchanged", and the answer's list reads as shorthand. The plan follows the design text: two files of one account at one address over one route stay valid, as today. Under the answer's literal text, `TestOneRouteManyUsersAccepted`'s entry `c` (user `ops`, same address, another `authorized_keys_file`) would be a load error instead. Keep entry `c` either way: if the main session picks the literal reading, the row moves to `TestOneAddressOneRoute` with the merge wording and `claimAddress` keys `targets` without `path`; nothing else in the plan changes.
18. **A hop failure in the login probe is "could not check".** `classifyProbe` gains a first branch: an error with a failed hop is `verdictHostKey` for a host-key kind and `verdictTransient` otherwise, never `verdictRejected`, whatever its kind or `AuthExhausted`. That is the design's "no restore, never `lockout_unrestored`", in P08's vocabulary (`unproven`).

## How this plan is cut (read first)

### Why the lanes look the way they do

1. **Three rounds.** The design's S5 row is A `internal/config`, `internal/fleet`, `internal/manifest/edit.go` · B `internal/sshconn`, `sshtest`, `internal/sshread`, `internal/sshwrite`, `internal/audit/{audit,report,json}.go` · C `internal/sshcfg`, `internal/reconcile`, `import.go` · D `internal/apply/{exec,plan,report,json}.go`, `adopt.go`, `sweep.go` · integrator `scripts/integration.sh`. B, C and D all read A's `via`/`jump_only`, so A is round 1. B and C own disjoint files and both build only on A: round 2. D needs B's `fleet.HopLimiter` (first caller `audit.Run`), `fleet.FailedHopOf` and the transport its E2E drives: round 3.
2. **`deadcode ./cmd/sshepherd` is in `make check`.** Every function round 1 adds is reachable from `config.Parse`: `ServerNamed`, `Chain` and `Dependents` through `validateVia`, `jumpOnly` through `Covers`/`DesiredFor`. `fleet.HopLimiter` and `fleet.FailedHopOf` therefore move from lane A to lane B, beside their first callers (`audit.Run`, `audit.tallyResults`); `authkeys.Forwards` lives in lane D beside `forwardRefusal`. Exported struct fields (`Server.Via`, `WriteRequest.ProbeForward`) are not functions and may land before their readers.
3. **`TestSchemaRoundTrip*` reflects over `config.Server`,** so the `Via` and `JumpOnly` samples in `cmd/sshepherd/schema_roundtrip_test.go` land in round 1 with the fields, or `make check` fails.
4. **The `DialHint` change crosses three packages in one commit** (gap 16), so lane B owns the apply goldens that mirror it in round 2, and D starts from them in round 3.
5. **`exitCodesWrite` is lane D's** (`cmd/sshepherd/run.go`), and `TestHelpExamplesAndExitCodes` reads it out of `README.md`, so lane D applies its README docsDelta locally for its own gate (Task 26 Step 4). The integrator applies the same delta before `make check`. Lane B also edits `run.go` (`logFleetReadDone`) in round 2; the rounds run in sequence, so no two lanes hold the file at once.
6. **`scripts/integration.sh` is lane B's** (gap 13); lane D's E2E uses the fixture accounts B adds and does not edit the script.

### Rounds

| Round | Lane (disjoint files) | Builds on |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `schema`: `internal/config`, `internal/manifest/edit_jump_test.go`, `cmd/sshepherd/schema_roundtrip_test.go`, `cmd/sshepherd/jump_schema_test.go` | `51e7ea0` |
| 2 | `transport`: `internal/fleet`, `internal/sshconn` (+`sshtest`), `internal/sshread`, `internal/sshwrite`, `internal/audit`, `internal/apply/report_test.go` and the 6 apply goldens carrying `DialHint`, `cmd/sshepherd/{fleet,apply,sweep,run}.go`, `cmd/sshepherd/bastion_hermetic_test.go`, `scripts/integration.sh` | round 1 |
| 2 | `import`: `internal/sshcfg`, `internal/reconcile`, `cmd/sshepherd/{import,import_json}.go` and their tests and goldens | round 1 |
| 3 | `writepath`: `internal/apply`, `internal/authkeys/forward*.go`, `internal/workflow`, `cmd/sshepherd/{adopt,sweep,run}.go`, `cmd/sshepherd/testdata/help/{apply,revoke,rotate}.golden`, `cmd/sshepherd/{jump_only_hermetic,bastion_e2e}_test.go` | round 2 |

After round 3 the integrator runs Task 30 and an independent verifier runs Task 31. No lane owns either.

### Conventions every lane follows

- Go runs under the pin: prefix every `go` command with `GOTOOLCHAIN=go1.27.3`. The rtk hook summarizes test output; use `rtk proxy go test …` when exact failure lines matter.
- A golden is rewritten only with `-update` and then read back line by line before it is accepted. Every `-update` run is followed by `git status --short -- '*testdata*'` (a new golden is untracked, so `git diff --stat` would not show it), which must list only the files the step names.
- **Failing first.** Every test is run and seen failing (build failure or assertion) before the code it tests exists; a test that pins behaviour already present says so in its step, and a build failure caused only by a sibling test does not count as seeing it fail.
- **gocyclo 15 is a production-code gate;** never `//nolint:gocyclo`. The blocks below split helpers where a function would cross it; keep the splits.
- **Run `gofmt -w` and `goimports -w` on every Go file you touch** before `make check`. Struct and comment alignment in these blocks is written by hand and was not machine-checked.
- **New test variables must not shadow builtins** (revive): no `new`, `len`, `max`, `min`, `copy`, `cap`, `close`, `clear`.
- **Hermetic tests own their goroutines.** Every `sshtest.Server`, agent and hop connection is closed by `t.Cleanup` or a `defer`; `goleak` (each package's `TestMain`) fails the package otherwise. A test that leaves a hop open is a real bug in the code under test (design: "failure paths close the hop, and goleak proves it").
- **A mutation run must finish before reporting.** Each lane's last task lists its mutants. Apply one at a time, run the named tests, record killed or survived, and revert. Report the table with the hand-back. Do not hand back while a mutant is still running, and do not report a survivor without saying why it is equivalent or which test you added to kill it.
- **A Fuzz target added or changed needs its seed corpus** under `<pkg>/testdata/fuzz/<Target>/` (at most 16 entries of 4 KiB or less per target).
- **Before hand-back:** run `make check` (and `make integration` under the runner's lock when the lane says `needsIntegration`; lanes `transport` and `writepath` run it for `IT_DISTRO=alpine`, and the integrator runs all three distros), then `git add -A`, unstage every path outside the lane's globs (`README.md` and `CLAUDE.md` always), and write the patch.
- **`docsDelta`** blocks are integrator-only text for `README.md`, `CLAUDE.md` Status and spec Status. A lane reports them unchanged and never edits those files in its patch (lane `writepath` applies its README delta locally for its own gate and unstages it).

## Round 1 — lane `schema` (Tasks 1–5)

Owns `internal/config/*`, `internal/manifest/edit_jump_test.go`, `cmd/sshepherd/schema_roundtrip_test.go`, `cmd/sshepherd/jump_schema_test.go`. `needsIntegration: false`.

**Files:**

```go
package config

import (
	"slices"
	"strings"
	"testing"

	"github.com/javadh75/SSHepherd/internal/testkeys"
)

// viaFleet: alice (k1); servers and access are the sections verbatim.
func viaFleet(t *testing.T, servers, access string) string {
	t.Helper()
	return "users:\n  - {name: alice, keys: [\"" + testkeys.Line(t, 1) + "\"]}\nservers:\n" + servers + "access:\n" + access
}

// loadErr parses y and returns its error text, failing when it loads.
func loadErr(t *testing.T, y string) string {
	t.Helper()
	_, err := Parse([]byte(y))
	if err == nil {
		t.Fatalf("Parse succeeded, want an error:\n%s", y)
	}
	return err.Error()
}

func TestViaValidation(t *testing.T) {
	for _, tt := range []struct {
		name, servers, want string
	}{
		{"unknown via", "  - {name: web-1, host: 10.0.1.5, user: deploy, via: nope}\n",
			`server "web-1": unknown via "nope"`},
		{"via names itself", "  - {name: web-1, host: 10.0.1.5, user: deploy, via: web-1}\n",
			`server "web-1": via names the server itself`},
		{"two-server loop", "  - {name: a, host: 10.0.0.1, user: u, via: b}\n  - {name: b, host: 10.0.0.2, user: u, via: a}\n",
			`server "a": via chain loops (a → b → a)`},
		{"four hops deep",
			"  - {name: e, host: 10.0.0.5, user: u}\n  - {name: d, host: 10.0.0.4, user: u, via: e}\n" +
				"  - {name: c, host: 10.0.0.3, user: u, via: d}\n  - {name: b, host: 10.0.0.2, user: u, via: c}\n" +
				"  - {name: a, host: 10.0.0.1, user: u, via: b}\n",
			`server "a": via chain is 4 hops deep (e → d → c → b); at most 3`},
	} {
		t.Run(tt.name, func(t *testing.T) {
			got := loadErr(t, viaFleet(t, tt.servers, "  - {user: alice, servers: [\"*\"]}\n"))
			if !strings.Contains(got, tt.want) {
				t.Errorf("error = %q, want it to contain %q", got, tt.want)
			}
		})
	}
}

// TestViaThreeHopsAccepted: exactly MaxViaDepth bastions load.
func TestViaThreeHopsAccepted(t *testing.T) {
	mustParse(t, viaFleet(t,
		"  - {name: d, host: 10.0.0.4, user: u}\n  - {name: c, host: 10.0.0.3, user: u, via: d}\n"+
			"  - {name: b, host: 10.0.0.2, user: u, via: c}\n  - {name: a, host: 10.0.0.1, user: u, via: b}\n",
		"  - {user: alice, servers: [\"*\"]}\n"))
}

func TestChainAndDependents(t *testing.T) {
	cfg := mustParse(t, viaFleet(t,
		"  - {name: edge, host: edge.example.com, user: jump, jump_only: true}\n"+
			"  - {name: mid, host: 10.0.0.2, user: ops, via: edge}\n"+
			"  - {name: web-1, host: 10.0.1.5, user: deploy, via: mid}\n"+
			"  - {name: web-2, host: 10.0.1.6, user: deploy, via: mid}\n"+
			"  - {name: db-1, host: 10.0.2.1, user: deploy}\n",
		"  - {user: alice, servers: [\"*\"]}\n"))
	for _, tt := range []struct {
		server string
		want   []string
	}{
		{"web-1", []string{"edge", "mid"}},
		{"mid", []string{"edge"}},
		{"edge", nil},
		{"db-1", nil},
	} {
		s, ok := cfg.ServerNamed(tt.server)
		if !ok {
			t.Fatalf("ServerNamed(%q) not found", tt.server)
		}
		if got := cfg.Chain(s); !slices.Equal(got, tt.want) {
			t.Errorf("Chain(%s) = %v, want %v (root first)", tt.server, got, tt.want)
		}
	}
	var deps []string
	for _, s := range cfg.Dependents("mid") {
		deps = append(deps, s.Name)
	}
	if !slices.Equal(deps, []string{"web-1", "web-2"}) {
		t.Errorf("Dependents(mid) = %v, want [web-1 web-2]", deps)
	}
	if _, ok := cfg.ServerNamed("nope"); ok {
		t.Error("ServerNamed found a server the manifest does not have")
	}
}
```

- [ ] **Step 4: The helpers.** Create `internal/config/via.go`:

```go
package config

import (
	"fmt"
	"slices"
	"strings"
)

// MaxViaDepth is the most bastions a server may sit behind (spec S5): a
// three-hop chain already uses most of the 30-second per-server budget.
const MaxViaDepth = 3

// ServerNamed returns the server called name.
func (c *Config) ServerNamed(name string) (Server, bool) {
	i := slices.IndexFunc(c.Servers, func(s Server) bool { return s.Name == name })
	if i < 0 {
		return Server{}, false
	}
	return c.Servers[i], true
}

// Chain lists the bastions s is reached through, root first: the one dialed
// directly leads and s's own via comes last. Nil for a direct server. It
// walks the whole fleet, so it answers for any server a run selects, and it
// stops after MaxViaDepth+1 names, so even an unvalidated manifest cannot
// make it loop.
func (c *Config) Chain(s Server) []string {
	var out []string
	for v := s.Via; v != "" && len(out) <= MaxViaDepth; {
		out = append(out, v)
		hop, ok := c.ServerNamed(v)
		if !ok {
			break
		}
		v = hop.Via
	}
	slices.Reverse(out)
	return out
}

// Dependents lists, in servers: order, every server whose via is name: the
// servers that bastion forwards to directly.
func (c *Config) Dependents(name string) []Server {
	var out []Server
	for _, s := range c.Servers {
		if s.Via == name {
			out = append(out, s)
		}
	}
	return out
}

// jumpOnly reports whether server is a jump_only bastion, which no grant
// reaches.
func (c *Config) jumpOnly(server string) bool {
	s, ok := c.ServerNamed(server)
	return ok && s.JumpOnly
}

// validateVia checks every via: (spec S5): it names another server of the
// manifest, its chain does not loop, and the chain is at most MaxViaDepth
// bastions long. A clean fleet then warns for each jump_only server that no
// via names.
func (c *Config) validateVia() []error {
	var errs []error
	for _, s := range c.Servers {
		_, known := c.ServerNamed(s.Via)
		switch {
		case s.Via == "":
		case s.Via == s.Name:
			errs = append(errs, fmt.Errorf("server %q: via names the server itself", s.Name))
		case !known:
			errs = append(errs, fmt.Errorf("server %q: unknown via %q", s.Name, s.Via))
		}
	}
	if len(errs) > 0 {
		return errs // a chain through an unknown name cannot be walked
	}
	for _, s := range c.Servers {
		if loop := c.viaLoop(s); loop != nil {
			errs = append(errs, fmt.Errorf("server %q: via chain loops (%s)", s.Name, strings.Join(loop, " → ")))
			continue
		}
		if chain := c.Chain(s); len(chain) > MaxViaDepth {
			errs = append(errs, fmt.Errorf("server %q: via chain is %d hops deep (%s); at most %d",
				s.Name, len(chain), strings.Join(chain, " → "), MaxViaDepth))
		}
	}
	if len(errs) == 0 {
		c.warnUnusedJumpOnly()
	}
	return errs
}

// viaLoop follows s's via chain and returns the walk up to the first name it
// repeats; nil when the chain ends.
func (c *Config) viaLoop(s Server) []string {
	path := []string{s.Name}
	seen := map[string]bool{s.Name: true}
	for v := s.Via; v != ""; {
		path = append(path, v)
		if seen[v] {
			return path
		}
		seen[v] = true
		hop, _ := c.ServerNamed(v) // known: validateVia checked every via first
		v = hop.Via
	}
	return nil
}

// warnUnusedJumpOnly records jump_only_unused for each jump_only server no
// via names: nothing connects through it, so nothing checks it either.
func (c *Config) warnUnusedJumpOnly() {
	for _, s := range c.Servers {
		if s.JumpOnly && len(c.Dependents(s.Name)) == 0 {
			c.warn(ManifestWarning{Kind: WarnJumpOnlyUnused, Server: s.Name, Message: fmt.Sprintf(
				"server %q: jump_only but no server has via: %s; nothing connects through it and nothing checks it", s.Name, s.Name)})
		}
	}
}
```

`jumpOnly` has no caller until Task 3 (same lane, same hand-back), so `deadcode` and `unused` stay green at the lane's `make check`.

```go
// TestOneAddressOneRoute (OP7): known_hosts is keyed by address, so one
// address is one entry, and every entry at one host:port takes one route.
func TestOneAddressOneRoute(t *testing.T) {
	bastions := "  - {name: b1, host: b1.example.com, user: jump}\n  - {name: b2, host: b2.example.com, user: jump}\n"
	route := `servers "a" and "b" both target deploy@10.0.0.5:22 ~/.ssh/authorized_keys through different hops; a manifest holds one entry per address, so remove one`
	for _, tt := range []struct {
		name, servers, want string
	}{
		{"one target, two hops", "  - {name: a, host: 10.0.0.5, user: deploy, via: b1}\n  - {name: b, host: 10.0.0.5, user: deploy, via: b2}\n", route},
		{"one target, direct and a hop", "  - {name: a, host: 10.0.0.5, user: deploy}\n  - {name: b, host: 10.0.0.5, user: deploy, via: b1}\n", route},
		{"one target, one hop", "  - {name: a, host: 10.0.0.5, user: deploy, via: b1}\n  - {name: b, host: 10.0.0.5, user: deploy, via: b1}\n",
			`servers "a" and "b" both target deploy@10.0.0.5:22 ~/.ssh/authorized_keys; merge them into one entry`},
		{"one address, two users, two hops", "  - {name: a, host: 10.0.0.5, user: deploy, via: b1}\n  - {name: b, host: 10.0.0.5, user: ops, via: b2}\n",
			`servers "a" and "b" reach 10.0.0.5:22 through different hops; every entry at one address must use the same route; if these are different machines, give each a hostname its own hop can resolve`},
		{"the address folds case", "  - {name: a, host: Web.Example.com, user: deploy}\n  - {name: b, host: web.example.com, user: ops, via: b1}\n",
			`servers "a" and "b" reach web.example.com:22 through different hops`},
	} {
		t.Run(tt.name, func(t *testing.T) {
			got := loadErr(t, viaFleet(t, bastions+tt.servers, "  - {user: alice, servers: [\"*\"]}\n"))
			if !strings.Contains(got, tt.want) {
				t.Errorf("error = %q, want it to contain %q", got, tt.want)
			}
		})
	}
}

}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement.** Replace `validateServers` (`internal/config/config.go:277-314`) with:

```go
func (c *Config) validateServers() []error {
	var errs []error
	seen := make(map[string]bool)
	targets := make(map[target]int) // target -> index of the first server
	routes := make(map[string]int)  // lower-cased host:port -> index of the first server there
	for i := range c.Servers {
		s := &c.Servers[i]
		if s.Name == "" {
			errs = append(errs, fmt.Errorf("servers[%d]: empty name", i))
			continue
		}
		if issue := ServerNameIssue(s.Name); issue != "" {
			// %q escapes the hostile runes so the error itself is inert.
			errs = append(errs, fmt.Errorf("servers[%d]: name %q %s", i, s.Name, issue))
			continue
		}
		if seen[s.Name] {
			errs = append(errs, fmt.Errorf("duplicate server %q", s.Name))
			continue
		}
		seen[s.Name] = true
		if fieldErrs := validateServerFields(s); len(fieldErrs) > 0 {
			errs = append(errs, fieldErrs...)
			continue // an invalid entry has no meaningful target
		}
		if err := c.claimAddress(i, targets, routes); err != nil {
			errs = append(errs, err)
		}
	}
	return errs
}

// claimAddress records server i's target and route (OP7): a second entry for
// one target is refused, worded by whether the two take the same route, and
// so is a second route to one host:port, whatever the user or file:
// known_hosts is keyed by address, so it could not tell two machines apart.
func (c *Config) claimAddress(i int, targets map[target]int, routes map[string]int) error {
	s := c.Servers[i]
	t := target{host: strings.ToLower(s.Host), port: s.Port, user: s.User, path: s.AuthorizedKeysFile}
	if t.path == "" {
		t.path = defaultAuthorizedKeysFile
	}
	if first, dup := targets[t]; dup {
		f := c.Servers[first]
		if f.Via != s.Via {
			return fmt.Errorf("servers %q and %q both target %s@%s:%d %s through different hops; a manifest holds one entry per address, so remove one",
				f.Name, s.Name, s.User, s.Host, s.Port, t.path)
		}
		return fmt.Errorf("servers %q and %q both target %s@%s:%d %s; merge them into one entry",
			f.Name, s.Name, s.User, s.Host, s.Port, t.path)
	}
	targets[t] = i
	addr := net.JoinHostPort(t.host, strconv.Itoa(s.Port))
	first, seen := routes[addr]
	switch {
	case !seen:
		routes[addr] = i
	case c.Servers[first].Via != s.Via:
		return fmt.Errorf("servers %q and %q reach %s through different hops; every entry at one address must use the same route; if these are different machines, give each a hostname its own hop can resolve",
			c.Servers[first].Name, s.Name, net.JoinHostPort(s.Host, strconv.Itoa(s.Port)))
	}
	return nil
}
```

Add `"net"` and `"strconv"` to the imports of `config.go`.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests.** Create `internal/config/jump_only_test.go`:

```go
package config

import (
	"reflect"
	"slices"
	"strings"
	"testing"

	"github.com/javadh75/SSHepherd/internal/testkeys"
)

// jumpFleet: alice (k1), op (k2, operator); edge is a jump_only bastion on
// a root account tagged prod and edge, and web-1 (prod) and db-1 (root) sit
// behind it.
func jumpFleet(t *testing.T, access string) string {
	t.Helper()
	return "users:\n  - {name: alice, keys: [\"" + testkeys.Line(t, 1) + "\"]}\n" +
		"  - {name: op, operator: true, keys: [\"" + testkeys.Line(t, 2) + "\"]}\n" +
		"servers:\n  - {name: edge, host: edge.example.com, user: root, jump_only: true, tags: [prod, edge]}\n" +
		"  - {name: web-1, host: 10.0.1.5, user: deploy, via: edge, tags: [prod]}\n" +
		"  - {name: db-1, host: 10.0.2.1, user: root, via: edge}\n" +
		"access:\n" + access
}

// TestJumpOnlyReach: "*", @tag and the operator's implicit grant skip a
// jump_only server; Covers is false for it; it renders nothing, so
// GrantCount does not count it and wildcard_root does not name it.
func TestJumpOnlyReach(t *testing.T) {
	cfg := mustParse(t, jumpFleet(t, "  - {user: alice, servers: [\"*\"]}\n  - {user: alice, servers: [\"@prod\"]}\n"))
	if got := cfg.DesiredFor("edge"); len(got) != 0 {
		t.Errorf("DesiredFor(edge) = %d keys, want none", len(got))
	}
	for _, user := range []string{"alice", "op"} {
		if cfg.Covers(user, "edge") {
			t.Errorf("Covers(%s, edge) = true, want false", user)
		}
	}
	if !hasKey(t, cfg, 1, "web-1") || !hasKey(t, cfg, 2, "db-1") {
		t.Error("the bastion's dependents lost their grants")
	}
	if got := cfg.GrantCount(); got != 4 {
		t.Errorf("GrantCount = %d, want 4 (alice and op on web-1 and db-1)", got)
	}
	for _, w := range cfg.ManifestWarnings {
		if w.Kind == WarnWildcardRoot && strings.Contains(w.Message, "edge") {
			t.Errorf("wildcard_root names the jump_only server: %s", w.Message)
		}
	}
}

// TestJumpOnlyGroupWildcard: a group's "*" skips a jump_only server too,
// so a tombstone naming the server blocks no group grant there and warns
// nothing about it.
func TestJumpOnlyGroupWildcard(t *testing.T) {
	y := strings.Replace(jumpFleet(t, "  - {group: team, servers: [\"*\"]}\n"), "servers:\n", "groups:\n  - {name: team, members: [alice]}\nservers:\n", 1) +
		"revoked:\n  - {user: alice, servers: [edge], date: 2026-11-10}\n"
	cfg := mustParse(t, y)
	if got := cfg.DesiredFor("edge"); len(got) != 0 {
		t.Errorf("DesiredFor(edge) = %d keys, want none", len(got))
	}
	if !hasKey(t, cfg, 1, "web-1") {
		t.Error("the group lost web-1")
	}
	for _, w := range cfg.ManifestWarnings {
		if w.Kind == WarnTombstoneBlocksGrant && w.Server == "edge" {
			t.Errorf("tombstone_blocks_grant names the jump_only server: %s", w.Message)
		}
	}
}

// TestJumpOnlyNotInConflictServers: a revoked operator key's conflict lists
// the servers whose render holds it; the implicit grant skips the bastion.
func TestJumpOnlyNotInConflictServers(t *testing.T) {
	cfg := mustParse(t, jumpFleet(t, "  - {user: alice, servers: [web-1]}\n")+
		"revoked:\n  - {fingerprint: "+fpOf(t, 2)+", date: 2026-11-10}\n")
	cs := cfg.Conflicts()
	if len(cs) != 1 || !slices.Equal(cs[0].Servers, []string{"web-1", "db-1"}) {
		t.Errorf("Conflicts = %+v, want one naming web-1 and db-1 only", cs)
	}
}

func TestJumpOnlyAccessErrors(t *testing.T) {
	for _, tt := range []struct {
		name, access, want string
	}{
		{"named in servers", "  - {user: alice, servers: [edge]}\n",
			`access for "alice": server "edge" is jump_only; access cannot name it`},
		{"named in except", "  - {user: alice, servers: [\"*\"], except: [edge]}\n",
			`access for "alice": server "edge" is jump_only; access cannot name it`},
		{"a tag only it carries", "  - {user: alice, servers: [\"@edge\"]}\n",
			`access for "alice": "@edge" matches only jump_only servers`},
		{"a tag nobody carries", "  - {user: alice, servers: [\"@nope\"]}\n",
			`access for "alice": unknown tag "@nope"`},
		{"a group names it", "  - {group: team, servers: [edge]}\n",
			`access for group "team": server "edge" is jump_only; access cannot name it`},
	} {
		t.Run(tt.name, func(t *testing.T) {
			y := strings.Replace(jumpFleet(t, tt.access), "servers:\n", "groups:\n  - {name: team, members: [alice]}\nservers:\n", 1)
			if got := loadErr(t, y); !strings.Contains(got, tt.want) {
				t.Errorf("error = %q, want it to contain %q", got, tt.want)
			}
		})
	}
}

func TestJumpOnlyWithAuthorizedKeysFile(t *testing.T) {
	y := "users: []\nservers:\n  - {name: edge, host: e, user: jump, jump_only: true, authorized_keys_file: /etc/k}\n" +
		"  - {name: web-1, host: w, user: deploy, via: edge}\n"
	if got := loadErr(t, y); !strings.Contains(got, `server "edge": jump_only has no managed file; remove authorized_keys_file`) {
		t.Errorf("error = %q", got)
	}
}

func TestJumpOnlyUnusedWarns(t *testing.T) {
	cfg := mustParse(t, "users: []\nservers:\n  - {name: bastion-eu, host: bastion.example.com, user: jump, jump_only: true}\n")
	want := ManifestWarning{Kind: WarnJumpOnlyUnused, Server: "bastion-eu",
		Message: `server "bastion-eu": jump_only but no server has via: bastion-eu; nothing connects through it and nothing checks it`}
	if len(cfg.ManifestWarnings) != 1 || !reflect.DeepEqual(cfg.ManifestWarnings[0], want) {
		t.Errorf("ManifestWarnings = %+v, want exactly %+v", cfg.ManifestWarnings, want)
	}
	used := mustParse(t, jumpFleet(t, "  - {user: alice, servers: [web-1]}\n"))
	for _, w := range used.ManifestWarnings {
		if w.Kind == WarnJumpOnlyUnused {
			t.Errorf("a bastion web-1 goes through warned: %s", w.Message)
		}
	}
}

// TestJumpOnlyTombstoneLoadsAndDoesNothing: a tombstone naming a jump_only
// server loads and has no effect while the flag is set; without the flag it
// applies again.
func TestJumpOnlyTombstoneLoadsAndDoesNothing(t *testing.T) {
	y := jumpFleet(t, "  - {user: alice, servers: [\"*\"]}\n") + "revoked:\n  - {user: alice, servers: [edge], date: 2026-11-10}\n"
	cfg := mustParse(t, y)
	if cs := cfg.Conflicts(); len(cs) != 0 {
		t.Errorf("Conflicts = %+v, want none", cs)
	}
	for _, w := range cfg.ManifestWarnings {
		if w.Kind == WarnTombstoneBlocksGrant {
			t.Errorf("a tombstone on a jump_only server blocked a grant: %s", w.Message)
		}
	}
	if !hasKey(t, cfg, 1, "web-1") {
		t.Error("alice lost web-1")
	}
	if cs := mustParse(t, strings.Replace(y, ", jump_only: true", "", 1)).Conflicts(); len(cs) != 1 {
		t.Errorf("without the flag: Conflicts = %+v, want alice's * over the tombstone", cs)
	}
}

// TestWildcardOverJumpOnlyAlone: a * entry over a fleet of jump_only
// servers grants nothing, the existing load error.
func TestWildcardOverJumpOnlyAlone(t *testing.T) {
	y := "users:\n  - {name: alice, keys: [\"" + testkeys.Line(t, 1) + "\"]}\n" +
		"servers:\n  - {name: edge, host: e, user: jump, jump_only: true}\naccess:\n  - {user: alice, servers: [\"*\"]}\n"
	if got := loadErr(t, y); !strings.Contains(got, `access for "alice": wildcard grants no servers`) {
		t.Errorf("error = %q", got)
	}
}
```

```go
// SelectorMatches reports whether selector item reaches server s: "*" reaches
// every server, "@tag" those carrying the tag, and neither reaches a
// jump_only server, which no grant may reach (spec S5, OP8). A server name
// is not a selector and matches nothing here.
func SelectorMatches(item string, s Server) bool {
	if s.JumpOnly {
		return false
	}
	if item == "*" {
		return true
	}
	tag, ok := strings.CutPrefix(item, tagSelectorPrefix)
	return ok && slices.Contains(s.Tags, tag)
}
```

That one predicate carries `expandServers`, `entryCovers`, `entryServers`, `warnWildcardRoot`'s targets, `GroupGrantsReaching`, `grantVia` and `manifest.selectorReach`.


Replace `entryShape` and `tagInUse` (`:477-512`) with:

```go
// entryShape is the access-entry rules that hold before any expansion: "*"
// stands alone, except: needs a selector and lists known server names none of
// which the entry also names, every @tag is carried by a server a grant can
// reach, and no name is a jump_only server.
func (c *Config) entryShape(a Access, servers map[string]bool) []error {
	if slices.Contains(a.Servers, "*") && len(a.Servers) != 1 {
		return []error{fmt.Errorf("access for %s: %q must be the only servers entry", entryLabel(a), "*")}
	}
	if len(a.Except) > 0 && !slices.ContainsFunc(a.Servers, IsSelector) {
		return []error{fmt.Errorf(`access for %s: except requires servers ["*"] or an @tag`, entryLabel(a))}
	}
	if len(a.Servers) == 0 {
		return []error{fmt.Errorf("access for %s: servers is empty (grants no servers)", entryLabel(a))}
	}
	errs := c.itemErrors(a)
	for _, e := range a.Except { // duplicates are tolerated, like grants unioning
		switch {
		case isTagSelector(e):
			errs = append(errs, fmt.Errorf("access for %s: except takes server names, not the selector %q", entryLabel(a), e))
		case !servers[e]:
			errs = append(errs, fmt.Errorf("access for %s: unknown except server %q", entryLabel(a), e))
		case slices.Contains(a.Servers, e):
			errs = append(errs, fmt.Errorf("access for %s: %q is in both servers and except", entryLabel(a), e))
		case c.jumpOnly(e):
			errs = append(errs, jumpOnlyNamed(a, e))
		}
	}
	return errs
}

// itemErrors checks an entry's servers: items: a @tag must be carried by a
// server, and by one that is not jump_only; a name must not be a jump_only
// server (spec S5, OP8).
func (c *Config) itemErrors(a Access) []error {
	var errs []error
	for _, item := range a.Servers {
		switch {
		case isTagSelector(item) && !c.tagCarried(item, true):
			errs = append(errs, fmt.Errorf("access for %s: unknown tag %q", entryLabel(a), item))
		case isTagSelector(item) && !c.tagCarried(item, false):
			errs = append(errs, fmt.Errorf("access for %s: %q matches only jump_only servers", entryLabel(a), item))
		case !IsSelector(item) && c.jumpOnly(item):
			errs = append(errs, jumpOnlyNamed(a, item))
		}
	}
	return errs
}

// jumpOnlyNamed is the refusal of an access entry naming a jump_only server.
func jumpOnlyNamed(a Access, server string) error {
	return fmt.Errorf("access for %s: server %q is jump_only; access cannot name it", entryLabel(a), server)
}

// tagCarried reports whether any server carries selector's tag; with
// withJumpOnly false, only a server a grant can reach counts.
func (c *Config) tagCarried(selector string, withJumpOnly bool) bool {
	tag := strings.TrimPrefix(selector, tagSelectorPrefix)
	return slices.ContainsFunc(c.Servers, func(s Server) bool {
		return slices.Contains(s.Tags, tag) && (withJumpOnly || !s.JumpOnly)
	})
}
```

```go
package manifest

import (
	"strings"
	"testing"

	"github.com/javadh75/SSHepherd/internal/testkeys"
)

// TestRemoveAccessDropsWildcardLeftOnJumpOnly: a "*" entry whose only other
// reach is jump_only servers is deleted once its last real server goes:
// selectorReach asks config.SelectorMatches, which skips jump_only (spec S5).
func TestRemoveAccessDropsWildcardLeftOnJumpOnly(t *testing.T) {
	y := "users:\n  - {name: bob, keys: [\"" + testkeys.Line(t, 1) + "\"]}\n" +
		"servers:\n  - {name: edge, host: e, user: jump, jump_only: true}\n  - {name: web-1, host: w, user: deploy, via: edge}\n" +
		"access:\n  - {user: bob, servers: [\"*\"]}\n"
	mf, err := Load([]byte(y))
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	r, err := mf.RemoveAccess("bob", []string{"web-1"})
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	if r.EntriesRemoved != 1 || r.WildcardsRemoved != 1 || r.ServersExcepted != 0 {
		t.Errorf("RemoveAccess = %+v, want the * entry deleted", r)
	}
	out, err := mf.Encode()
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	if strings.Contains(string(out), `"*"`) || strings.Contains(string(out), "except") {
		t.Errorf("the * entry survived:\n%s", out)
	}
}
```

```go
package main

import (
	"reflect"
	"strings"

// TestAdoptRegenerateKeepsJumpOnly: the round-trip sample keeps JumpOnly
// false, so this pins that a jump_only bastion and its dependent's via
// survive generateAdoptManifest (spec S5 "Manifest tooling").
func TestAdoptRegenerateKeepsJumpOnly(t *testing.T) {
	cfg, _, _, _ := roundTripConfig(t)
	cfg.Servers = []config.Server{
		{Name: "bastion-eu", Host: "bastion.example.com", Port: 22, User: "jump", JumpOnly: true},
		{Name: "web-1", Host: "10.0.1.5", Port: 22, User: "deploy", Via: "bastion-eu"},
	}
	cfg.Access = []config.Access{{User: "bob", Servers: []string{"web-1"}}}
	out, err := generateAdoptManifest(cfg, adopt.Plan{}, "rt.yaml", nil)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	got, err := config.Parse(out)
	if err != nil || !reflect.DeepEqual(got.Servers, cfg.Servers) {
		t.Errorf("adopt changed the servers (err %v):\n%s", err, out)
	}
}

// TestValidateJumpOnly: validate counts a jump_only server, warns that its
// host key is not recorded, and reports jump_only_unused (exit 0, 1 under
// --strict).
func TestValidateJumpOnly(t *testing.T) {
	path := writeConfig(t, "users:\n  - {name: alice, keys: [\""+testkeys.Line(t, 1)+"\"]}\n"+
		"servers:\n  - {name: bastion-eu, host: 10.9.0.1, user: jump, jump_only: true}\n  - {name: web-1, host: 10.0.1.5, user: deploy, via: bastion-eu}\n"+
		"access:\n  - {user: alice, servers: [\"*\"]}\n")
	code, out, stderr := validateCLI(t, "--config", path, "--known-hosts", recordedKnownHosts(t, "10.0.1.5"))
	if code != 0 || out != "manifest OK: 1 user, 2 servers, 1 grant (1 warning)\n" || !strings.Contains(stderr, "bastion-eu") {
		t.Fatalf("exit = %d, stdout = %q, stderr = %q", code, out, stderr)
	}
	unused := writeConfig(t, "users: []\nservers:\n  - {name: bastion-eu, host: 10.9.0.1, user: jump, jump_only: true}\n")
	code, out, _ = validateCLI(t, "--strict", "--json", "--config", unused, "--known-hosts", recordedKnownHosts(t, "10.9.0.1"))
	if code != 1 {
		t.Fatalf("--strict: exit = %d, want 1", code)
	}
	ws, _ := oneJSONDoc(t, out)["manifest_warnings"].([]any)
	want := map[string]any{"kind": "jump_only_unused", "server": "bastion-eu",
		"message": `server "bastion-eu": jump_only but no server has via: bastion-eu; nothing connects through it and nothing checks it`}
	if len(ws) != 1 || !reflect.DeepEqual(ws[0], want) {
		t.Errorf("manifest_warnings = %v, want exactly %v", ws, want)
	}
}
```

```go
package fleet

import (
	"context"
	"errors"
	"fmt"
	"testing"
	"time"
)

type hopErr struct{ hop string }

func (e hopErr) Error() string     { return "via " + e.hop + ": refused" }
func (e hopErr) FailedHop() string { return e.hop }

func TestFailedHopOf(t *testing.T) {
	if got := FailedHopOf(fmt.Errorf("read web-1: %w", hopErr{"bastion-eu"})); got != "bastion-eu" {
		t.Errorf("FailedHopOf(wrapped) = %q, want bastion-eu", got)
	}
	if got := FailedHopOf(errors.New("plain")); got != "" {
		t.Errorf("FailedHopOf(plain) = %q, want empty", got)
	}
	if got := FailedHopOf(nil); got != "" {
		t.Errorf("FailedHopOf(nil) = %q, want empty", got)
	}
}

func acquireOK(t *testing.T, l *HopLimiter, chain []string, n int) func() {
	t.Helper()
	ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), 5*time.Second)
	defer cancel()
	release, err := l.Acquire(ctx, chain, n)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("Acquire(%v, %d): %v", chain, n, err)
	}
	return release
}

// TestHopLimiterCap (OP2): HopCap holders of one bastion, never more; a
// waiter gets the first released slot; another bastion is unaffected.
func TestHopLimiterCap(t *testing.T) {
	var l HopLimiter
	var releases []func()
	for range HopCap {
		releases = append(releases, acquireOK(t, &l, []string{"edge"}, 1))
	}
	got := make(chan func(), 1)
	go func() {
		release, err := l.Acquire(context.Background(), []string{"edge"}, 1)
		if err != nil {
			release = func() {}
		}
		got <- release
	}()
	select {
	case <-got:
		t.Fatal("a ninth connection through one bastion got a slot")
	case <-time.After(50 * time.Millisecond):
	}
	acquireOK(t, &l, []string{"other"}, 1)()
	releases[0]()
	select {
	case release := <-got:
		release()
	case <-time.After(5 * time.Second):
		t.Fatal("the waiter never got the released slot")
	}
	for _, release := range releases[1:] {
		release()
	}
}

// TestHopLimiterTakesBothSlotsTogether: a write takes its two slots as one,
// so with one slot left it waits instead of holding half, and the free slot
// stays free for a read.
func TestHopLimiterTakesBothSlotsTogether(t *testing.T) {
	var l HopLimiter
	var releases []func()
	for range HopCap - 1 {
		releases = append(releases, acquireOK(t, &l, []string{"edge"}, 1))
	}
	ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), 50*time.Millisecond)
	defer cancel()
	if _, err := l.Acquire(ctx, []string{"edge"}, 2); !errors.Is(err, context.DeadlineExceeded) {
		t.Fatalf("two-slot Acquire with one slot free = %v, want it to wait out its context", err)
	}
	acquireOK(t, &l, []string{"edge"}, 1)()
	for _, release := range releases {
		release()
	}
}

// TestHopLimiterCancelReleasesWhatItTook: canceled while waiting on the
// second bastion of a chain, Acquire gives the first one's slot back.
func TestHopLimiterCancelReleasesWhatItTook(t *testing.T) {
	var l HopLimiter
	var releases []func()
	for range HopCap {
		releases = append(releases, acquireOK(t, &l, []string{"mid"}, 1))
	}
	ctx, cancel := context.WithCancel(context.Background())
	time.AfterFunc(20*time.Millisecond, cancel)
	if _, err := l.Acquire(ctx, []string{"edge", "mid"}, 1); !errors.Is(err, context.Canceled) {
		t.Fatalf("Acquire = %v, want context.Canceled", err)
	}
	for range HopCap {
		releases = append(releases, acquireOK(t, &l, []string{"edge"}, 1))
	}
	for _, release := range releases {
		release()
	}
}

// TestHopLimiterEmptyChain: a direct server takes nothing.
func TestHopLimiterEmptyChain(t *testing.T) {
	var l HopLimiter
	acquireOK(t, &l, nil, 2)()
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement.** Create `internal/fleet/hop.go`:

```go
package fleet

import (
	"context"
	"errors"
	"fmt"
	"sync"
)

// HopCap is the most connections SSHepherd keeps open through one bastion at
// a time (OP2): below sshd's default MaxStartups 10:30:100, so a fan-out
// never trips the bastion's own throttling. A constant, with no flag.
const HopCap = 8

// HopFailure is implemented by connection errors that failed at a bastion
// (sshconn.Error with HopStage set): reaching it, or its refusal to forward.
// Domain code reads it through FailedHopOf, never the transport type.
type HopFailure interface {
	error
	FailedHop() string
}

// FailedHopOf names the bastion at which err's connection failed: the JSON
// reports' via_failed. "" when err did not fail at a bastion.
func FailedHopOf(err error) string {
	var h HopFailure
	if errors.As(err, &h) {
		return h.FailedHop()
	}
	return ""
}

// HopLimiter caps the connections in flight through each bastion at HopCap.
// audit.Run and apply.Execute take a server's slots at the fan-out, before
// its per-server budget starts, so waiting for a slot never uses up its
// timeout. Use it by pointer; the zero value is ready.
type HopLimiter struct {
	mu   sync.Mutex
	used map[string]int
	wake chan struct{} // closed on every release; nil while nobody waits
}

// Acquire takes n slots on every bastion of chain, root first as
// config.Config.Chain lists it, waiting while one is full, and returns the
// release. The n slots of one bastion are taken together, and every caller
// walks its chain in the same root-first order, so two callers can never
// each hold part of what the other needs. An empty chain takes nothing.
// When ctx ends during a wait, Acquire gives back what it took and returns
// an error wrapping ctx's.
func (l *HopLimiter) Acquire(ctx context.Context, chain []string, n int) (func(), error) {
	var held []string
	for _, hop := range chain {
		if err := l.take(ctx, hop, n); err != nil {
			l.release(held, n)
			return nil, err
		}
		held = append(held, hop)
	}
	return func() { l.release(held, n) }, nil
}

// take waits until n slots of hop are free and takes them.
func (l *HopLimiter) take(ctx context.Context, hop string, n int) error {
	for {
		l.mu.Lock()
		if l.used == nil {
			l.used = make(map[string]int)
		}
		if l.used[hop]+n <= HopCap {
			l.used[hop] += n
			l.mu.Unlock()
			return nil
		}
		if l.wake == nil {
			l.wake = make(chan struct{})
		}
		wake := l.wake
		l.mu.Unlock()
		select {
		case <-wake:
		case <-ctx.Done():
			return fmt.Errorf("wait for a slot on bastion %s: %w", hop, ctx.Err())
		}
	}
}

// release gives n slots of each hop back and wakes every waiter.
func (l *HopLimiter) release(hops []string, n int) {
	if len(hops) == 0 {
		return
	}
	l.mu.Lock()
	defer l.mu.Unlock()
	for _, hop := range hops {
		l.used[hop] -= n
	}
	if l.wake != nil {
		close(l.wake)
		l.wake = nil
	}
}
```

```go
	// writer opens a direct-tcpip channel there and closes it at once; a
	// prohibited channel is a rejected login check (restore). "" skips it.
```

```go
// Forwards lists the host:port of every direct-tcpip channel clients asked
// for, in order, as they sent it (unresolved).
func (s *Server) Forwards() []string {
	s.mu.Lock()
	defer s.mu.Unlock()
	return append([]string(nil), s.forwards...)
```

```go
// DialForward is a Forward that dials host:port from the test process: a
// bastion that reaches every other test server. The host is resolved here,
// on the "bastion", never by the client.
func DialForward(host string, port uint32) (net.Conn, ssh.RejectionReason) {
	d := net.Dialer{Timeout: 5 * time.Second}
	c, err := d.DialContext(context.Background(), "tcp", net.JoinHostPort(host, strconv.FormatUint(uint64(port), 10)))
	if err != nil {
		return nil, ssh.ConnectionFailed
	}
	return c, 0
}

// ProhibitForward is a Forward that refuses every channel, as sshd does
// with AllowTcpForwarding no or a no-port-forwarding key line.
func ProhibitForward(string, uint32) (net.Conn, ssh.RejectionReason) { return nil, ssh.Prohibited }
```

```go
// forwardMsg is the direct-tcpip open payload (RFC 4254 7.2).
type forwardMsg struct {
	Host     string
	Port     uint32
	OrigHost string
	OrigPort uint32
}

// serveForward records the requested address, asks Forward, and splices
// the channel to the connection it returns until either side ends.
func (s *Server) serveForward(nc ssh.NewChannel, wg *sync.WaitGroup) {
	var m forwardMsg
	if err := ssh.Unmarshal(nc.ExtraData(), &m); err != nil {
		_ = nc.Reject(ssh.ConnectionFailed, "sshtest: bad direct-tcpip payload")
		return
	}
	s.mu.Lock()
	s.forwards = append(s.forwards, net.JoinHostPort(m.Host, strconv.FormatUint(uint64(m.Port), 10)))
	s.mu.Unlock()
	conn, reason := s.forward(m.Host, m.Port)
	if conn == nil {
		_ = nc.Reject(reason, "sshtest: forward refused")
		return
	}
	ch, reqs, err := nc.Accept()
	if err != nil {
		_ = conn.Close()
		return
	}
	wg.Add(2)
	go func() {
		defer wg.Done()
		ssh.DiscardRequests(reqs)
	}()
	go func() {
		defer wg.Done()
		splice(ch, conn)
	}()
}

// splice copies both ways until either side ends, then closes both and
// waits for the other copy.
func splice(ch ssh.Channel, conn net.Conn) {
	done := make(chan struct{}, 2)
	go func() { _, _ = io.Copy(ch, conn); done <- struct{}{} }()
	go func() { _, _ = io.Copy(conn, ch); done <- struct{}{} }()
	<-done
	_ = ch.Close()
	_ = conn.Close()
	<-done
}
```

```go
// DialHint is the operator remediation for a TCP connect that failed. It is
// carried in Error.Hint, never in the message, so reports print it once per
// run and JSON keeps it in a separate `hint` field.
const DialHint = "check the host, port, and firewall; a host reachable only through a bastion needs via: in the manifest"

// ForwardHint is the remediation when a bastion refused to open the
// direct-tcpip channel (sshd's AllowTcpForwarding no, or a no-port-forwarding,
// restrict or permitopen= key line).
const ForwardHint = "the bastion refused the forward; allow TCP forwarding for this account"

// HopDialHint is the remediation when the bastion opened the channel but
// could not reach the server behind it (gap item 2): the server's own
// failure, so it carries no HopStage, and DialHint's "needs via:" advice
// would be wrong for a server that already has one.
const HopDialHint = "the bastion could not reach this server; check that it is up and reachable from the bastion"
```

```go
package sshconn

import (
	"context"
	"fmt"
	"io"
	"net"
	"slices"
	"strconv"
	"strings"
	"sync"
	"testing"
	"time"

	"golang.org/x/crypto/ssh"
	"golang.org/x/crypto/ssh/knownhosts"

	"github.com/javadh75/SSHepherd/internal/config"
	"github.com/javadh75/SSHepherd/internal/fleet"
	"github.com/javadh75/SSHepherd/internal/sshconn/sshtest"
)

// hopFixture: a bastion that forwards (DialForward) and a target, both
// accepting key 1 from the agent; known_hosts records both; the Dialer
// resolves "bastion".
type hopFixture struct {
	hop, target       *sshtest.Server
	hopSrv, targetSrv config.Server
	hopKey, targetKey ssh.Signer
	agent, kh         string
}

func newHopFixture(t *testing.T, hopCfg, targetCfg sshtest.ServerConfig) hopFixture {
	t.Helper()
	pub := sshtest.Signer(t, sshtest.Key(1)).PublicKey()
	f := hopFixture{hopKey: sshtest.Signer(t, sshtest.Key(40)), targetKey: sshtest.Signer(t, sshtest.Key(41))}
	if hopCfg.HostKeys == nil {
		hopCfg.HostKeys = []ssh.Signer{f.hopKey}
	}
	if hopCfg.Authorized == nil {
		hopCfg.Authorized = []ssh.PublicKey{pub}
	}
	if hopCfg.Forward == nil {
		hopCfg.Forward = sshtest.DialForward
	}
	if targetCfg.HostKeys == nil {
		targetCfg.HostKeys = []ssh.Signer{f.targetKey}
	}
	if targetCfg.Authorized == nil {
		targetCfg.Authorized = []ssh.PublicKey{pub}
	}
	f.hop, f.target = sshtest.NewServer(t, hopCfg), sshtest.NewServer(t, targetCfg)
	f.hopSrv = config.Server{Name: "bastion", Host: f.hop.Host, Port: f.hop.Port, User: "jump"}
	f.targetSrv = config.Server{Name: "web-1", Host: f.target.Host, Port: f.target.Port, User: "deploy", Via: "bastion"}
	f.agent = sshtest.Agent(t, sshtest.Key(1))
	f.kh = sshtest.KnownHostsFile(t, f.hop.KnownHostsLine(f.hopKey.PublicKey()), f.target.KnownHostsLine(f.targetKey.PublicKey()))
	return f
}

func (f hopFixture) dialer() *Dialer {
	return &Dialer{KnownHostsPath: f.kh, Creds: &Credentials{AgentSock: f.agent}, DialTimeout: 5 * time.Second,
		Server: func(name string) (config.Server, bool) {
			if name == f.hopSrv.Name {
				return f.hopSrv, true
			}
			return config.Server{}, false
		}}
}

// TestConnectThroughHop: the target is reached over a direct-tcpip channel
// the bastion opened; the bastion runs nothing; Close tears both down.
func TestConnectThroughHop(t *testing.T) {
	f := newHopFixture(t, sshtest.ServerConfig{}, sshtest.ServerConfig{Handle: sshtest.ShellHandler(t.TempDir())})
	conn, err := f.dialer().Connect(ctxTimeout(t, 10*time.Second), f.targetSrv)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("Connect: %v", err)
	}
	code, stdout, _, err := Exec(context.Background(), conn.Client, `echo through`, nil)
	if err != nil || code != 0 || stdout.String() != "through\n" {
		t.Fatalf("Exec = (%d, %q, %v)", code, stdout.String(), err)
	}
	if err := conn.Close(); err != nil {
		t.Errorf("Close: %v", err)
	}
	if got := f.hop.Forwards(); !slices.Equal(got, []string{f.target.Addr()}) {
		t.Errorf("bastion forwards = %v, want [%s]", got, f.target.Addr())
	}
	if cmds := f.hop.Commands(); len(cmds) != 0 {
		t.Errorf("the bastion ran %q; a hop runs nothing", cmds)
	}
}

// TestConnectThroughHopPassesHostnameToBastion: DNS for the target is the
// bastion's: the client sends the hostname unresolved (spec S5, fact 3).
func TestConnectThroughHopPassesHostnameToBastion(t *testing.T) {
	f := newHopFixture(t, sshtest.ServerConfig{}, sshtest.ServerConfig{})
	f.targetSrv.Host = "localhost"
	addr := net.JoinHostPort("localhost", strconv.Itoa(f.target.Port))
	f.kh = sshtest.KnownHostsFile(t, f.hop.KnownHostsLine(f.hopKey.PublicKey()), knownhosts.Line([]string{addr}, f.targetKey.PublicKey()))
	conn, err := f.dialer().Connect(ctxTimeout(t, 10*time.Second), f.targetSrv)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("Connect: %v", err)
	}
	_ = conn.Close()
	if got := f.hop.Forwards(); !slices.Equal(got, []string{addr}) {
		t.Errorf("bastion was asked for %v, want the unresolved %s", got, addr)
	}
}

// TestConnectViaWithoutLookupFailsClosed: a via server on a Dialer that
// cannot resolve its bastion is never dialed directly.
func TestConnectViaWithoutLookupFailsClosed(t *testing.T) {
	f := newHopFixture(t, sshtest.ServerConfig{}, sshtest.ServerConfig{})
	d := f.dialer()
	d.Server = nil
	_, err := d.Connect(ctxTimeout(t, 5*time.Second), f.targetSrv)
	if err == nil || !strings.Contains(err.Error(), "refusing to dial") {
		t.Fatalf("Connect = %v, want the fail-closed refusal", err)
	}
	if len(f.target.Attempts())+len(f.hop.Attempts()) != 0 {
		t.Error("a via server was dialed without its bastion")
	}
}

// TestConnectHopFailures: a failure at the bastion keeps its kind and hint,
// names the bastion, and never reaches the target.
func TestConnectHopFailures(t *testing.T) {
	t.Run("unreachable bastion", func(t *testing.T) {
		f := newHopFixture(t, sshtest.ServerConfig{}, sshtest.ServerConfig{})
		l, err := (&net.ListenConfig{}).Listen(context.Background(), "tcp", "127.0.0.1:0")
		if err != nil {
			t.Fatal(err)
		}
		f.hopSrv.Port = l.Addr().(*net.TCPAddr).Port
		_ = l.Close()
		_, err = f.dialer().Connect(ctxTimeout(t, 5*time.Second), f.targetSrv)
		assertHopErr(t, err, fleet.ErrorKindDial, "bastion", DialHint)
		if !strings.HasPrefix(err.Error(), "via bastion: ") {
			t.Errorf("error = %q, want the via prefix", err)
		}
	})
	t.Run("changed bastion host key", func(t *testing.T) {
		f := newHopFixture(t, sshtest.ServerConfig{}, sshtest.ServerConfig{})
		f.kh = sshtest.KnownHostsFile(t, f.hop.KnownHostsLine(sshtest.Signer(t, sshtest.Key(42)).PublicKey()),
			f.target.KnownHostsLine(f.targetKey.PublicKey()))
		_, err := f.dialer().Connect(ctxTimeout(t, 5*time.Second), f.targetSrv)
		if kindOf(t, err) != fleet.ErrorKindHostKeyChanged || fleet.FailedHopOf(err) != "bastion" {
			t.Fatalf("err = %v (kind %q, hop %q), want host_key_changed at bastion", err, kindOf(t, err), fleet.FailedHopOf(err))
		}
		if len(f.target.Attempts()) != 0 || len(f.hop.Attempts()) != 0 {
			t.Error("credentials were offered past a changed bastion key")
		}
	})
	t.Run("forward prohibited", func(t *testing.T) {
		f := newHopFixture(t, sshtest.ServerConfig{Forward: sshtest.ProhibitForward}, sshtest.ServerConfig{})
		_, err := f.dialer().Connect(ctxTimeout(t, 5*time.Second), f.targetSrv)
		assertHopErr(t, err, fleet.ErrorKindDial, "bastion", ForwardHint)
	})
	t.Run("target the bastion cannot reach", func(t *testing.T) {
		down := func(string, uint32) (net.Conn, ssh.RejectionReason) { return nil, ssh.ConnectionFailed }
		f := newHopFixture(t, sshtest.ServerConfig{Forward: down}, sshtest.ServerConfig{})
		_, err := f.dialer().Connect(ctxTimeout(t, 5*time.Second), f.targetSrv)
		assertHopErr(t, err, fleet.ErrorKindDial, "", HopDialHint)
	})
	// A jump_only bastion's host key is still strict (spec S5): unrecorded,
	// @revoked or of another type, it stops the tunnel before any key is
	// offered, and the failure names the bastion.
	t.Run("unrecorded jump_only bastion key", func(t *testing.T) {
		f := newHopFixture(t, sshtest.ServerConfig{}, sshtest.ServerConfig{})
		f.hopSrv.JumpOnly = true
		f.kh = sshtest.KnownHostsFile(t, f.target.KnownHostsLine(f.targetKey.PublicKey()))
		_, err := f.dialer().Connect(ctxTimeout(t, 5*time.Second), f.targetSrv)
		if kindOf(t, err) != fleet.ErrorKindHostKeyUnknown || fleet.FailedHopOf(err) != "bastion" {
			t.Fatalf("err = %v (kind %q, hop %q), want host_key_unknown at bastion", err, kindOf(t, err), fleet.FailedHopOf(err))
		}
		if len(f.target.Attempts()) != 0 || len(f.hop.Attempts()) != 0 {
			t.Error("credentials were offered past an unrecorded bastion key")
		}
		// The bastion itself has no route, so its scan is the direct form.
		hint := fleet.HintOf(err)
		if want := "ssh-keyscan -t ed25519 -p " + strconv.Itoa(f.hop.Port) + " '127.0.0.1' | ssh-keygen -lf -"; !strings.Contains(hint, want) ||
			strings.Contains(hint, "as seen by the bastion") {
			t.Errorf("hint = %q, want the direct scan %q", hint, want)
		}
	})
	t.Run("revoked jump_only bastion key", func(t *testing.T) {
		f := newHopFixture(t, sshtest.ServerConfig{}, sshtest.ServerConfig{})
		f.hopSrv.JumpOnly = true
		f.kh = sshtest.KnownHostsFile(t, f.hop.KnownHostsLine(f.hopKey.PublicKey()),
			"@revoked * "+strings.TrimSpace(string(ssh.MarshalAuthorizedKey(f.hopKey.PublicKey()))),
			f.target.KnownHostsLine(f.targetKey.PublicKey()))
		_, err := f.dialer().Connect(ctxTimeout(t, 5*time.Second), f.targetSrv)
		if kindOf(t, err) != fleet.ErrorKindHostKeyRevoked || fleet.FailedHopOf(err) != "bastion" {
			t.Fatalf("err = %v (kind %q, hop %q), want host_key_revoked at bastion", err, kindOf(t, err), fleet.FailedHopOf(err))
		}
	})
	t.Run("jump_only bastion key of another type", func(t *testing.T) {
		// known_hosts records the ed25519 hopKey; the bastion presents ECDSA.
		f := newHopFixture(t, sshtest.ServerConfig{HostKeys: []ssh.Signer{ecdsaSigner(t)}}, sshtest.ServerConfig{})
		f.hopSrv.JumpOnly = true
		_, err := f.dialer().Connect(ctxTimeout(t, 5*time.Second), f.targetSrv)
		if kindOf(t, err) != fleet.ErrorKindHostKeyTypeChanged || fleet.FailedHopOf(err) != "bastion" {
			t.Fatalf("err = %v (kind %q, hop %q), want host_key_type_changed at bastion", err, kindOf(t, err), fleet.FailedHopOf(err))
		}
		if len(f.target.Attempts()) != 0 {
			t.Error("the target was reached past a changed bastion key type")
		}
	})
	t.Run("target refuses the key", func(t *testing.T) {
		f := newHopFixture(t, sshtest.ServerConfig{},
			sshtest.ServerConfig{Authorized: []ssh.PublicKey{sshtest.Signer(t, sshtest.Key(9)).PublicKey()}})
		_, err := f.dialer().Connect(ctxTimeout(t, 5*time.Second), f.targetSrv)
		assertHopErr(t, err, fleet.ErrorKindAuth, "", "")
		// goleak (TestMain) fails the package if the hop stayed open.
	})
}

func assertHopErr(t *testing.T, err error, kind fleet.ErrorKind, hop, hint string) {
	t.Helper()
	if err == nil {
		t.Fatal("Connect succeeded")
	}
	if got := kindOf(t, err); got != kind {
		t.Errorf("kind = %q, want %q (err: %v)", got, kind, err)
	}
	if got := fleet.FailedHopOf(err); got != hop {
		t.Errorf("FailedHopOf = %q, want %q", got, hop)
	}
	if got := fleet.HintOf(err); got != hint {
		t.Errorf("hint = %q, want %q", got, hint)
	}
}

// TestConnectThroughHopDeadlineAndCancel: the hop's socket bounds a
// stalled target; Ctrl-C returns at once, even with the tunnel wedged.
func TestConnectThroughHopDeadlineAndCancel(t *testing.T) {
	t.Run("stalled target times out", func(t *testing.T) {
		f := newHopFixture(t, sshtest.ServerConfig{}, sshtest.ServerConfig{Stall: true})
		start := time.Now()
		_, err := f.dialer().Connect(ctxTimeout(t, 300*time.Millisecond), f.targetSrv)
		if kindOf(t, err) != fleet.ErrorKindTimeout || time.Since(start) > 2*time.Second {
			t.Fatalf("err = %v after %v, want timeout within ~300ms", err, time.Since(start))
		}
	})
	t.Run("cancel with a wedged tunnel", func(t *testing.T) {
		// The freeze must land after the tunnel is open: the target reads the
		// client's first byte (its SSH version line, sent only once
		// CHANNEL_OPEN_CONFIRMATION arrived), then the proxy in front of the
		// bastion freezes and ctx is canceled. Closing the channel alone now
		// waits for a close reply the frozen proxy never delivers; only
		// closing the hop returns.
		ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), 20*time.Second)
		defer cancel()
		proxy, freeze := frozenProxy(t)
		f := newHopFixture(t, sshtest.ServerConfig{}, sshtest.ServerConfig{})
		f.targetSrv.Port = stallAfterHello(t, func() { freeze(); cancel() })
		proxy.to(f.hop.Addr())
		f.hopSrv.Port = proxy.port
		f.kh = sshtest.KnownHostsFile(t, knownhosts.Line([]string{proxy.addr()}, f.hopKey.PublicKey()),
			f.target.KnownHostsLine(f.targetKey.PublicKey()))
		start := time.Now()
		_, err := f.dialer().Connect(ctx, f.targetSrv)
		if kindOf(t, err) != fleet.ErrorKindCanceled || time.Since(start) > 2*time.Second {
			t.Fatalf("err = %v after %v, want canceled at once (closing the channel alone waits for a reply that never comes)", err, time.Since(start))
		}
	})
	t.Run("cancel with a wedged bastion", func(t *testing.T) {
		f := newHopFixture(t, sshtest.ServerConfig{Stall: true}, sshtest.ServerConfig{})
		ctx, cancel := context.WithCancel(context.Background())
		time.AfterFunc(100*time.Millisecond, cancel)
		start := time.Now()
		_, err := f.dialer().Connect(ctx, f.targetSrv)
		if kindOf(t, err) != fleet.ErrorKindCanceled || fleet.FailedHopOf(err) != "bastion" || time.Since(start) > 2*time.Second {
			t.Fatalf("err = %v after %v, want canceled at the bastion at once", err, time.Since(start))
		}
	})
}

// TestConnectThroughHopOutlivesItsConnectContext: the connectForWrite
// pattern — the connect ctx is released at once and its deadline passes,
// and SessionTimeout moved the hop's socket deadline, so the tunnelled
// session still runs (gap item 1).
func TestConnectThroughHopOutlivesItsConnectContext(t *testing.T) {
	f := newHopFixture(t, sshtest.ServerConfig{}, sshtest.ServerConfig{Handle: sshtest.ShellHandler(t.TempDir())})
	ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), time.Second)
	conn, err := f.dialer().ConnectWith(ctx, f.targetSrv, ConnectOptions{SessionTimeout: 20 * time.Second})
	cancel()
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("ConnectWith: %v", err)
	}
	defer func() { _ = conn.Close() }()
	time.Sleep(1500 * time.Millisecond) // past the connect deadline the hop's socket started with
	code, stdout, _, err := Exec(context.Background(), conn.Client, `echo alive`, nil)
	if err != nil || code != 0 || stdout.String() != "alive\n" {
		t.Fatalf("Exec after the connect deadline = (%d, %q, %v), want the session alive", code, stdout.String(), err)
	}
}

// TestConnCloseClosesHopWithNilClient: Close always closes the hop.
func TestConnCloseClosesHopWithNilClient(t *testing.T) {
	f := newHopFixture(t, sshtest.ServerConfig{}, sshtest.ServerConfig{})
	hop, err := f.dialer().Connect(ctxTimeout(t, 5*time.Second), f.hopSrv)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	if err := (&Conn{hop: hop}).Close(); err != nil {
		t.Errorf("Close = %v", err)
	}
	if _, err := hop.Client.NewSession(); err == nil {
		t.Error("the hop is still open after Close")
	}
}

// TestHopOffering: a jump_only bastion is offered every held key in
// Signers order with no fallback warning; a managed one only its granted
// keys (D17).
func TestHopOffering(t *testing.T) {
	k1, k2 := sshtest.Key(1), sshtest.Key(2)
	fp2 := sshtest.Fingerprint(t, k2)
	for _, tt := range []struct {
		name     string
		jumpOnly bool
		want     func(fp1 string) []string
	}{
		{"jump_only", true, func(fp1 string) []string { return []string{fp1, fp2} }},
		{"managed", false, func(string) []string { return []string{fp2} }},
	} {
		t.Run(tt.name, func(t *testing.T) {
			f := newHopFixture(t, sshtest.ServerConfig{Authorized: []ssh.PublicKey{sshtest.Signer(t, k2).PublicKey()}},
				sshtest.ServerConfig{Authorized: []ssh.PublicKey{sshtest.Signer(t, k2).PublicKey()}})
			f.agent = sshtest.Agent(t, k1, k2)
			f.hopSrv.JumpOnly = tt.jumpOnly
			d := f.dialer()
			buf, log := logBuffer()
			d.Log = log
			d.PreferKeys = func(s config.Server) []string {
				if s.JumpOnly {
					return nil
				}
				return []string{fp2}
			}
			conn, err := d.Connect(ctxTimeout(t, 10*time.Second), f.targetSrv)
			if err != nil {
				t.Fatalf("Connect: %v", err)
			}
			_ = conn.Close()
			if got, want := f.hop.Attempts(), tt.want(sshtest.Fingerprint(t, k1)); !slices.Equal(got, want) {
				t.Errorf("bastion was offered %v, want %v", got, want)
			}
			if strings.Contains(buf.String(), "none of the keys granted here") {
				t.Errorf("the hop warned about a fallback:\n%s", buf.String())
			}
		})
	}
}

// TestHopOfferingFallsBack (D17): a managed bastion none of whose granted
// keys the run holds is offered every held key with the fallback warning;
// QuietFallback (after the preflight warned once) drops it to debug.
func TestHopOfferingFallsBack(t *testing.T) {
	k1, k2 := sshtest.Key(1), sshtest.Key(2)
	fp1, fp2 := sshtest.Fingerprint(t, k1), sshtest.Fingerprint(t, k2)
	unheld := sshtest.Fingerprint(t, sshtest.Key(9))
	for _, quiet := range []bool{false, true} {
		t.Run(fmt.Sprintf("quiet=%v", quiet), func(t *testing.T) {
			f := newHopFixture(t, sshtest.ServerConfig{Authorized: []ssh.PublicKey{sshtest.Signer(t, k2).PublicKey()}},
				sshtest.ServerConfig{Authorized: []ssh.PublicKey{sshtest.Signer(t, k2).PublicKey()}})
			f.agent = sshtest.Agent(t, k1, k2)
			d := f.dialer()
			buf, log := logBuffer()
			d.Log, d.QuietFallback = log, quiet
			d.PreferKeys = func(s config.Server) []string {
				if s.Name == f.hopSrv.Name {
					return []string{unheld}
				}
				return []string{fp2}
			}
			conn, err := d.Connect(ctxTimeout(t, 10*time.Second), f.targetSrv)
			if err != nil {
				t.Fatalf("Connect: %v", err)
			}
			_ = conn.Close()
			if got := f.hop.Attempts(); !slices.Equal(got, []string{fp1, fp2}) {
				t.Errorf("bastion was offered %v, want every held key %v", got, []string{fp1, fp2})
			}
			warned := strings.Contains(buf.String(), "bastion: none of the keys granted here are loaded")
			if warned == quiet {
				t.Errorf("fallback warning at info = %v with QuietFallback %v:\n%s", warned, quiet, buf.String())
			}
		})
	}
}

// TestHopIgnoresTargetOnly (spec S5 Transport): the hop never inherits the
// target's Only or Prefer: the login probe's IdentitiesOnly offer is the
// target's, and the hop still offers as any connection to it does.
func TestHopIgnoresTargetOnly(t *testing.T) {
	k1, k2 := sshtest.Key(1), sshtest.Key(2)
	fp1, fp2 := sshtest.Fingerprint(t, k1), sshtest.Fingerprint(t, k2)
	f := newHopFixture(t, sshtest.ServerConfig{Authorized: []ssh.PublicKey{sshtest.Signer(t, k2).PublicKey()}},
		sshtest.ServerConfig{Authorized: []ssh.PublicKey{sshtest.Signer(t, k1).PublicKey()}})
	f.agent = sshtest.Agent(t, k1, k2)
	conn, err := f.dialer().ConnectWith(ctxTimeout(t, 10*time.Second), f.targetSrv, ConnectOptions{Only: []string{fp1}, Prefer: []string{fp1}})
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("ConnectWith(Only: key 1): %v (the hop must not be limited to the target's keys)", err)
	}
	_ = conn.Close()
	if got := f.hop.Attempts(); !slices.Contains(got, fp2) {
		t.Errorf("bastion was offered %v, want key 2 among them", got)
	}
}

// stallAfterHello is a raw TCP target: on each connection it reads one
// byte, calls hello, and then stalls (reads to EOF) until the test ends. It
// returns the port. Cleanup closes the listener and every connection and
// waits for its goroutines (goleak).
func stallAfterHello(t *testing.T, hello func()) int {
	t.Helper()
	l, err := (&net.ListenConfig{}).Listen(context.Background(), "tcp", "127.0.0.1:0")
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	var (
		wg     sync.WaitGroup
		mu     sync.Mutex
		conns  []net.Conn
		closed bool
	)
	t.Cleanup(func() {
		_ = l.Close()
		mu.Lock()
		closed = true
		for _, c := range conns {
			_ = c.Close()
		}
		mu.Unlock()
		wg.Wait()
	})
	wg.Add(1)
	go func() {
		defer wg.Done()
		for {
			c, err := l.Accept()
			if err != nil {
				return
			}
			mu.Lock()
			if closed {
				mu.Unlock()
				_ = c.Close()
				return
			}
			conns = append(conns, c)
			wg.Add(1)
			mu.Unlock()
			go func() {
				defer wg.Done()
				if _, err := c.Read(make([]byte, 1)); err == nil {
					hello()
				}
				_, _ = io.Copy(io.Discard, c)
			}()
		}
	}()
	return l.Addr().(*net.TCPAddr).Port
}

// frozenTCP is a TCP proxy in front of the bastion that stops forwarding,
// both ways, once frozen: a bastion wedged mid-tunnel.
type frozenTCP struct {
	l      net.Listener
	port   int
	frozen chan struct{}
	once   sync.Once
	mu     sync.Mutex
	conns  []net.Conn
	wg     sync.WaitGroup
	target string
}

func frozenProxy(t *testing.T) (*frozenTCP, func()) {
	t.Helper()
	l, err := (&net.ListenConfig{}).Listen(context.Background(), "tcp", "127.0.0.1:0")
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	p := &frozenTCP{l: l, port: l.Addr().(*net.TCPAddr).Port, frozen: make(chan struct{})}
	t.Cleanup(func() {
		_ = l.Close()
		p.mu.Lock()
		for _, c := range p.conns {
			_ = c.Close()
		}
		p.mu.Unlock()
		p.wg.Wait()
	})
	return p, func() { p.once.Do(func() { close(p.frozen) }) }
}

func (p *frozenTCP) addr() string { return net.JoinHostPort("127.0.0.1", strconv.Itoa(p.port)) }

// to starts proxying every accepted connection to target.
func (p *frozenTCP) to(target string) {
	p.target = target
	p.wg.Add(1)
	go func() {
		defer p.wg.Done()
		for {
			c, err := p.l.Accept()
			if err != nil {
				return
			}
			up, err := (&net.Dialer{}).DialContext(context.Background(), "tcp", p.target)
			if err != nil {
				_ = c.Close()
				continue
			}
			p.mu.Lock()
			p.conns = append(p.conns, c, up)
			p.mu.Unlock()
			p.wg.Add(2)
			go func() { defer p.wg.Done(); p.pipe(up, c) }()
			go func() { defer p.wg.Done(); p.pipe(c, up) }()
		}
	}()
}

// pipe copies src to dst until either ends or the proxy freezes; once
// frozen it drops what it read and stops, leaving both sockets open.
func (p *frozenTCP) pipe(dst, src net.Conn) {
	buf := make([]byte, 32*1024)
	for {
		n, err := src.Read(buf)
		if n > 0 {
			select {
			case <-p.frozen:
				return
			default:
			}
			if _, werr := dst.Write(buf[:n]); werr != nil {
				return
			}
		}
		if err != nil {
			return
		}
	}
}
```

Replace `Conn` and `Close` (`:113-138`) with:

```go
// Conn is an authenticated SSH connection to one server.
type Conn struct {
	Client  *ssh.Client
	session *sessionKey
	// hop is the bastion connection this one is tunnelled through (via:);
	// nil for a direct connection. Close closes it after the target.
	hop *Conn
	// raw is the transport under Client: the TCP socket, or for a tunnelled
	// connection the direct-tcpip channel, whose SetDeadline does nothing;
	// setDeadline moves the hop's socket along with it.
	raw net.Conn
}

// Close closes the connection and then, always, its hop, even when Client
// is nil. A bare Conn (a test fake) closes to nil.
func (c *Conn) Close() error {
	var err error
	if c.Client != nil {
		if cerr := c.Client.Close(); cerr != nil {
			err = fmt.Errorf("close ssh connection: %w", cerr)
		}
	}
	c.hop.closeQuiet()
	return err
}

// closeQuiet closes c when it is not nil and drops the error: a hop is the
// tunnel's carrier, and its close error is never the caller's concern.
func (c *Conn) closeQuiet() {
	if c != nil {
		_ = c.Close()
	}
}

// setDeadline moves the socket deadline of c and of every hop under it.
func (c *Conn) setDeadline(t time.Time) {
	if c.raw != nil {
		_ = c.raw.SetDeadline(t)
	}
	if c.hop != nil {
		c.hop.setDeadline(t)
	}
}
```

```go
	log.Debug("dialing", "server", srv.Name, "addr", addr, "via", srv.Via)

	if err != nil {
```
