```go
package authkeys

import (
	"strconv"
	"strings"
)

// Forwards reports whether a key line with these options lets its session
// open a direct-tcpip channel to host:port, the ssh -J that a via: server
// relies on (spec S5): no no-port-forwarding, no restrict unless
// port-forwarding re-enables it, and, when the line carries permitopen=
// clauses, one names that host (or "*") and that port (or "*"). Hosts
// compare as sshd compares them: literally, with no patterns and no name
// lookup.
func Forwards(opts []string, host string, port int) bool {
	restricted, reenabled := false, false
	var permits []string
	for _, o := range opts {
		switch nameOf(o) {
		case "no-port-forwarding":
			return false
		case "restrict":
			restricted = true
		case "port-forwarding":
			reenabled = true
		case "permitopen":
			_, v, _ := strings.Cut(o, "=")
			permits = append(permits, strings.Trim(v, `"`))
		}
	}
	if restricted && !reenabled {
		return false
	}
	if len(permits) == 0 {
		return true
	}
	for _, p := range permits {
		if permitMatches(p, host, port) {
			return true
		}
	}
	return false
}

// permitMatches reports whether one permitopen="host:port" admits
// host:port; an IPv6 host is bracketed.
func permitMatches(spec, host string, port int) bool {
	h, p, ok := cutPermit(spec)
	return ok && (h == "*" || h == host) && (p == "*" || p == strconv.Itoa(port))
}

func cutPermit(spec string) (host, port string, ok bool) {
	if rest, bracketed := strings.CutPrefix(spec, "["); bracketed {
		return strings.Cut(rest, "]:")
	}
	i := strings.LastIndex(spec, ":")
	if i < 0 {
		return "", "", false
	}
	return spec[:i], spec[i+1:], true
}
```

```go
package apply

import (
	"context"
	"strings"
	"testing"

	"github.com/javadh75/SSHepherd/internal/audit"
	"github.com/javadh75/SSHepherd/internal/config"
	"github.com/javadh75/SSHepherd/internal/fleet"
	"github.com/javadh75/SSHepherd/internal/testkeys"
)

// filesReader serves every server the same file content; nobody is
// unreachable.
type filesReader map[string]string

func (f filesReader) ReadAuthorizedKeys(_ context.Context, s config.Server) (fleet.ReadResult, error) {
	return fleet.ReadResult{Content: []byte(f[s.Name])}, nil
}

// bastionPlans plans manifest y with every server's file empty, holding
// key 1 (agent).
func bastionPlans(t *testing.T, y string) (*config.Config, []ServerPlan) {
	t.Helper()
	cfg, err := config.Parse([]byte(y))
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("Parse: %v\n%s", err, y)
	}
	results := audit.Run(context.Background(), cfg, filesReader{}, audit.Options{Parallel: 4})
	held := []fleet.HeldKey{{Fingerprint: testKey(t, 1).Fingerprint, Source: fleet.SourceAgent}}
	plans, err := BuildWith(cfg, results, held, BuildOptions{})
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	return cfg, plans
}

func planNamed(t *testing.T, plans []ServerPlan, name string) ServerPlan {
	t.Helper()
	for _, p := range plans {
		if p.Server.Name == name {
			return p
		}
	}
	t.Fatalf("no plan for %s", name)
	return ServerPlan{}
}

// bastionYAML: op (key 1, operator) with opts on its key line; a managed
// bastion-eu with web-1 and web-2 behind it.
func bastionYAML(t *testing.T, opts string) string {
	t.Helper()
	return "users:\n  - {name: op, operator: true, keys: ['" + opts + testkeys.Line(t, 1) + "']}\n" +
		"servers:\n  - {name: bastion-eu, host: bastion.example.com, user: jump}\n" +
		"  - {name: web-1, host: 10.0.1.5, user: deploy, via: bastion-eu}\n" +
		"  - {name: web-2, host: 10.0.1.6, user: deploy, via: bastion-eu}\n"
}

// TestForwardGuard (spec S5 Guard): a managed bastion must keep forwarding
// to every dependent for a key this run holds.
func TestForwardGuard(t *testing.T) {
	const refusal = "none of the surviving keys you hold may forward through bastion-eu (no-port-forwarding, restrict or permitopen=); this write would cut off web-1 and 1 more"
	for _, tt := range []struct {
		name, opts string
		refused    bool
	}{
		{"a plain line", "", false},
		{"no-port-forwarding", "no-port-forwarding ", true},
		{"restrict", "restrict ", true},
		{"restrict with port-forwarding", "restrict,port-forwarding ", false},
		{"permitopen for one dependent only", `permitopen="10.0.1.5:22" `, true},
		{"permitopen for both", `permitopen="10.0.1.5:22",permitopen="10.0.1.6:22" `, false},
	} {
		t.Run(tt.name, func(t *testing.T) {
			_, plans := bastionPlans(t, bastionYAML(t, tt.opts))
			p := planNamed(t, plans, "bastion-eu")
			switch {
			case tt.refused && (p.Class != ClassRefused || p.Refusal != refusal):
				t.Errorf("bastion plan = %s %q, want refused with %q", p.Class, p.Refusal, refusal)
			case !tt.refused && p.Class != ClassChange:
				t.Errorf("bastion plan = %s %q, want a change", p.Class, p.Refusal)
			}
			if w := planNamed(t, plans, "web-1"); w.Class != ClassChange {
				t.Errorf("web-1 = %s %q: a dependent has no forwarding guard", w.Class, w.Refusal)
			}
		})
	}
}

// TestPlanSetsProbeForward: a bastion's write probes a forward to its first
// dependent; a server nothing goes through probes none.
func TestPlanSetsProbeForward(t *testing.T) {
	_, plans := bastionPlans(t, bastionYAML(t, ""))
	if got := planNamed(t, plans, "bastion-eu").ProbeForward; got != "10.0.1.5:22" {
		t.Errorf("bastion ProbeForward = %q, want 10.0.1.5:22", got)
	}
	if got := planNamed(t, plans, "web-1").ProbeForward; got != "" {
		t.Errorf("web-1 ProbeForward = %q, want none", got)
	}
}

// TestPlanJumpOnly: a jump_only server is its own class, never guarded,
// never probed, and never changes the exit code.
func TestPlanJumpOnly(t *testing.T) {
	y := strings.Replace(bastionYAML(t, "no-port-forwarding "), "user: jump}", "user: jump, jump_only: true}", 1)
	_, plans := bastionPlans(t, y)
	p := planNamed(t, plans, "bastion-eu")
	if p.Class != ClassJumpOnly || p.Refusal != "" || p.ProbeForward != "" {
		t.Errorf("bastion plan = %+v, want class jump_only with nothing else", p)
	}
	for i := range plans {
		if plans[i].Class == ClassChange {
			plans[i].Outcome = OutcomeApplied
		}
	}
	if code := ExitCode(plans); code != 0 {
		t.Errorf("ExitCode = %d, want 0 with the dependents applied", code)
	}
}

// hopAlarm is a host-key alarm at a bastion (apply cannot import sshconn).
type hopAlarm struct{ hop string }

func (e hopAlarm) Error() string              { return "via " + e.hop + ": host key changed" }
func (e hopAlarm) ErrorKind() fleet.ErrorKind { return fleet.ErrorKindHostKeyChanged }
func (e hopAlarm) FailedHop() string          { return e.hop }

// TestNeedsHumanIgnoresBastionHostKey: an UNPROVEN login check stopped by
// a bastion's host-key alarm is exit 1; the server's own is still 2 (D10b).
func TestNeedsHumanIgnoresBastionHostKey(t *testing.T) {
	unproven := func(last error) []ServerPlan {
		return []ServerPlan{{Class: ClassChange, Outcome: OutcomeUnproven, OutcomeErr: &fleet.UnprovenError{Attempts: 1, HostKey: true, Last: last}}}
	}
	if code := ExitCode(unproven(hopAlarm{"bastion-eu"})); code != 1 {
		t.Errorf("bastion alarm: ExitCode = %d, want 1", code)
	}
	if code := ExitCode(unproven(hopAlarm{""})); code != 2 {
		t.Errorf("the server's own alarm: ExitCode = %d, want 2", code)
	}
}

// hopDown is a bastion that stopped answering during the login check.
type hopDown struct{ hop string }

func (e hopDown) Error() string              { return "via " + e.hop + ": connection refused" }
func (e hopDown) ErrorKind() fleet.ErrorKind { return fleet.ErrorKindDial }
func (e hopDown) FailedHop() string          { return e.hop }

// TestHopDeathInProbeIsExit1 (spec S5: "no restore, exit 1, never
// lockout_unrestored"): an UNPROVEN login check whose last failure was the
// bastion going away is exit 1. It pins P08 behaviour the S5 change must
// keep; sshwrite's TestProbeHopDeathIsUnproven proves the writer returns
// exactly this shape (UNPROVEN, no LockoutError, file kept).
func TestHopDeathInProbeIsExit1(t *testing.T) {
	plans := []ServerPlan{{Class: ClassChange, Outcome: OutcomeUnproven,
		OutcomeErr: &fleet.UnprovenError{Attempts: 4, Last: hopDown{"b"}}}}
	if code := ExitCode(plans); code != 1 {
		t.Errorf("ExitCode = %d, want 1", code)
	}
}
```

```go
// forwardRefusal is the bastion guard (spec S5): a managed server another
// server's via names must keep forwarding for a key this run holds, so some
// surviving key's line must allow a direct-tcpip channel to every
// dependent. "" when one does, or when nothing goes through the server. The
// post-write forward probe then checks the key that actually logs in (gap
// item 3).
func forwardRefusal(cfg *config.Config, server string, surviving []authkeys.Key) string {
	deps := cfg.Dependents(server)
	if len(deps) == 0 {
		return ""
	}
	for _, k := range surviving {
		if forwardsAll(k.Options, deps) {
			return ""
		}
	}
	more := ""
	if n := len(deps) - 1; n > 0 {
		more = fmt.Sprintf(" and %d more", n)
	}
	return fmt.Sprintf("none of the surviving keys you hold may forward through %s (no-port-forwarding, restrict or permitopen=); this write would cut off %s%s",
		server, deps[0].Name, more)
}

// forwardsAll reports whether a key line may forward to every dependent.
func forwardsAll(opts []string, deps []config.Server) bool {
	for _, d := range deps {
		if !authkeys.Forwards(opts, d.Host, d.Port) {
			return false
		}
	}
	return true
}

// probeForwardFor is where the bastion's post-write probe forwards: its
// first dependent, as the bastion will be asked for it; "" when nothing
// goes through the server.
func probeForwardFor(cfg *config.Config, server string) string {
	deps := cfg.Dependents(server)
	if len(deps) == 0 {
		return ""
	}
	return net.JoinHostPort(deps[0].Host, strconv.Itoa(deps[0].Port))
}
```

```go
// TestRenderBastionGoldens: the jump only row and summary segment, the via
// head, JSON class jump_only with outcome null, via, summary.jump_only; and
// the forwarding refusal.
func TestRenderBastionGoldens(t *testing.T) {
	y := strings.Replace(bastionYAML(t, ""), "user: jump}", "user: jump, jump_only: true}", 1)
	cfg, plans := bastionPlans(t, y)
	var text, js bytes.Buffer
	Render(&text, cfg, plans, RenderOptions{})
	RenderSummary(&text, cfg, plans, RenderOptions{})
	checkGolden(t, "plan_jump_only.golden", text.Bytes())
	if err := RenderJSON(&js, cfg, plans, JSONOptions{Command: "apply", DryRun: true}); err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	checkGolden(t, "json_jump_only.golden.json", js.Bytes())

	cfg, plans = bastionPlans(t, bastionYAML(t, "no-port-forwarding "))
	text.Reset()
	Render(&text, cfg, plans, RenderOptions{})
	RenderSummary(&text, cfg, plans, RenderOptions{})
	checkGolden(t, "plan_forward_refused.golden", text.Bytes())

	// via_failed: an unreachable server's phase-1 read and a change whose
	// login check stopped at the bastion both name it.
	cfg, _ = bastionPlans(t, y)
	web1, _ := cfg.ServerNamed("web-1")
	web2, _ := cfg.ServerNamed("web-2")
	failed := []ServerPlan{
		{Server: web1, Class: ClassUnreachable, Err: hopAlarm{"bastion-eu"}},
		{Server: web2, Class: ClassChange, Outcome: OutcomeUnproven,
			OutcomeErr: &fleet.UnprovenError{Attempts: 4, Last: fmt.Errorf("login check: %w", hopDown{"bastion-eu"})}},
	}
	js.Reset()
	if err := RenderJSON(&js, cfg, failed, JSONOptions{Command: "apply"}); err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	checkGolden(t, "json_via_failed.golden.json", js.Bytes())
	if n := strings.Count(js.String(), `"via_failed": "bastion-eu"`); n != 2 {
		t.Errorf("via_failed appears %d times, want on both servers:\n%s", n, js.String())
	}
}
```

```go
// viaFailedOf is the bastion where the server's connection failed: the
// phase-1 read's for an unreachable server, the write's for a change.
func viaFailedOf(p ServerPlan) string {
	switch p.Class {
	case ClassUnreachable:
		return fleet.FailedHopOf(p.Err)
	case ClassChange:
		return fleet.FailedHopOf(p.OutcomeErr)
	}
	return ""
}
```

```go
package apply

import (
	"context"
	"errors"
	"fmt"
	"slices"
	"sync"
	"sync/atomic"
	"testing"
	"time"

	"github.com/javadh75/SSHepherd/internal/config"
	"github.com/javadh75/SSHepherd/internal/fleet"
)

// orderWriter records start and end of every write, in one sequence, and
// can fail or hold chosen servers.
type orderWriter struct {
	mu        sync.Mutex
	events    []string
	fail      map[string]bool
	hold      time.Duration
	now, peak atomic.Int32
}

func (w *orderWriter) Apply(_ context.Context, srv config.Server, _ fleet.WriteRequest) error {
	w.add("start " + srv.Name)
	n := w.now.Add(1)
	for { // raise peak to n (revive: no empty loop body)
		p := w.peak.Load()
		if n <= p || w.peak.CompareAndSwap(p, n) {
			break
		}
	}
	time.Sleep(w.hold)
	w.now.Add(-1)
	w.add("end " + srv.Name)
	if w.fail[srv.Name] {
		return errors.New("write failed")
	}
	return nil
}

func (w *orderWriter) add(e string) {
	w.mu.Lock()
	defer w.mu.Unlock()
	w.events = append(w.events, e)
}

func (w *orderWriter) index(e string) int {
	w.mu.Lock()
	defer w.mu.Unlock()
	return slices.Index(w.events, e)
}

// chainOf is a Chain over fixed bastion lists.
func chainOf(chains map[string][]string) func(config.Server) []string {
	return func(s config.Server) []string { return chains[s.Name] }
}

func levelPlans(t *testing.T, names ...string) []ServerPlan {
	t.Helper()
	k := testKey(t, 1)
	plans := make([]ServerPlan, 0, len(names))
	for _, n := range names {
		plans = append(plans, changePlan(n, k))
	}
	return plans
}

func levelOpts(t *testing.T, chain func(config.Server) []string) ExecOptions {
	t.Helper()
	return ExecOptions{Parallel: 32, Chain: chain, Held: []fleet.HeldKey{{Fingerprint: testKey(t, 1).Fingerprint, Source: fleet.SourceAgent}}}
}

// TestWriteLevels (spec S5 "Apply write order"): A via B via C writes A,
// then B, then C, a level at a time; a converged middle hop still keeps C
// after A; a failed dependent does not stop its bastion; a jump_only
// bastion is in no level.
func TestWriteLevels(t *testing.T) {
	chains := map[string][]string{"a": {"c", "b"}, "b": {"c"}}
	t.Run("dependents first, deepest bastion last", func(t *testing.T) {
		w := &orderWriter{hold: 20 * time.Millisecond}
		Execute(context.Background(), levelPlans(t, "c", "b", "a"), w, levelOpts(t, chainOf(chains)))
		if !(w.index("end a") < w.index("start b") && w.index("end b") < w.index("start c")) {
			t.Errorf("events = %v, want a, then b, then c", w.events)
		}
	})
	t.Run("a converged middle hop", func(t *testing.T) {
		w := &orderWriter{hold: 20 * time.Millisecond}
		plans := levelPlans(t, "c", "b", "a")
		plans[1].Class = ClassConverged
		Execute(context.Background(), plans, w, levelOpts(t, chainOf(chains)))
		if !(w.index("end a") < w.index("start c")) || w.index("start b") >= 0 {
			t.Errorf("events = %v, want a then c, and b untouched", w.events)
		}
	})
	t.Run("a failed dependent does not stop its bastion", func(t *testing.T) {
		w := &orderWriter{fail: map[string]bool{"a": true}}
		plans := levelPlans(t, "c", "b", "a")
		Execute(context.Background(), plans, w, levelOpts(t, chainOf(chains)))
		if plans[2].Outcome != OutcomeFailed || plans[1].Outcome != OutcomeApplied || plans[0].Outcome != OutcomeApplied {
			t.Errorf("outcomes = %s %s %s, want c and b applied after a failed", plans[0].Outcome, plans[1].Outcome, plans[2].Outcome)
		}
	})
	t.Run("a jump_only bastion is in no level", func(t *testing.T) {
		w := &orderWriter{}
		plans := levelPlans(t, "edge", "web")
		plans[0].Class = ClassJumpOnly
		Execute(context.Background(), plans, w, levelOpts(t, chainOf(map[string][]string{"web": {"edge"}})))
		if w.index("start edge") >= 0 || w.index("start web") < 0 {
			t.Errorf("events = %v, want web only", w.events)
		}
	})
}

// TestExecuteTwoSlotWrites (OP2): a write takes two bastion slots (its
// session and its login check), so at most fleet.HopCap/2 writes run
// through one bastion at once.
func TestExecuteTwoSlotWrites(t *testing.T) {
	names := []string{"w1", "w2", "w3", "w4", "w5", "w6", "w7", "w8"}
	chains := map[string][]string{}
	for _, n := range names {
		chains[n] = []string{"edge"}
	}
	w := &orderWriter{hold: 30 * time.Millisecond}
	Execute(context.Background(), levelPlans(t, names...), w, levelOpts(t, chainOf(chains)))
	if p := w.peak.Load(); p > fleet.HopCap/2 || p < 2 {
		t.Errorf("peak writes through edge = %d, want 2..%d", p, fleet.HopCap/2)
	}
}

// TestExecuteSlotsCoverTheWholeChain: a write behind mid1 or mid2 takes
// its two slots on edge too (the whole chain, root first), so the writes in
// flight through edge stay at fleet.HopCap/2 however they split between the
// inner bastions. Server.Via is set as the manifest would, so a fan-out that
// read the plan's own via instead of the chain would let 8 writes through.
func TestExecuteSlotsCoverTheWholeChain(t *testing.T) {
	chains := map[string][]string{}
	var names []string
	for i := range fleet.HopCap {
		for _, mid := range []string{"mid1", "mid2"} {
			n := fmt.Sprintf("%s-w%d", mid, i)
			names = append(names, n)
			chains[n] = []string{"edge", mid}
		}
	}
	plans := levelPlans(t, names...)
	for i := range plans {
		plans[i].Server.Via = chains[plans[i].Server.Name][1]
	}
	w := &orderWriter{hold: 30 * time.Millisecond}
	Execute(context.Background(), plans, w, levelOpts(t, chainOf(chains)))
	if p := w.peak.Load(); p > fleet.HopCap/2 {
		t.Errorf("peak writes through edge = %d, want at most %d", p, fleet.HopCap/2)
	}
}

// TestExecutePassesProbeForward: the plan's forward target reaches the
// writer.
func TestExecutePassesProbeForward(t *testing.T) {
	plans := levelPlans(t, "bastion-eu")
	plans[0].ProbeForward = "10.0.1.5:22"
	f := &fakeWriter{}
	Execute(context.Background(), plans, f, levelOpts(t, nil))
	if got := f.calls["bastion-eu"].ProbeForward; got != "10.0.1.5:22" {
		t.Errorf("ProbeForward = %q, want 10.0.1.5:22", got)
	}
}

// TestExecuteCancelBetweenLevels: Ctrl-C during the dependents' level
// leaves the bastion pending, never started.
func TestExecuteCancelBetweenLevels(t *testing.T) {
	ctx, cancel := context.WithCancel(context.Background())
	plans := levelPlans(t, "b", "a")
	f := &fakeWriter{onCall: func(string) { cancel() }}
	Execute(ctx, plans, f, levelOpts(t, chainOf(map[string][]string{"a": {"b"}})))
	if _, wrote := f.calls["b"]; wrote || Interrupted(plans) != 1 {
		t.Errorf("calls = %v, Interrupted = %d; want only a written and b pending", f.calls, Interrupted(plans))
	}
}
```

```go
// slowFirstWriter records writes; a's write is slow, so without levels b

type slowFirstWriter struct{ r *rec }

func (w slowFirstWriter) Apply(_ context.Context, s config.Server, _ fleet.WriteRequest) error {
```

Replace `Execute` (`:48-91`) with:

```go
// Execute writes every ClassChange plan through w with a bounded pool,
// mutating each plan's Outcome in place; other classes are untouched.
// Servers proceed independently — no global abort: partial application is
// visible in the report and re-running apply is always safe because the
// render is idempotent and every write is conditional on its plan's
// snapshot. Writes run in levels (writeLevels): a server before the
// bastions it is reached through, each level finished before the next, so
// a bastion's write can never strand its dependents' pending writes. A
// write behind bastions takes two fleet.HopLimiter slots on each of them
// first. Once ctx is canceled no new server starts (those stay pending with
// OutcomeErr set), while a server whose write already began finishes its
// write, verify and login probe.
func Execute(ctx context.Context, plans []ServerPlan, w Writer, opts ExecOptions) {
	if opts.Parallel < 1 {
		opts.Parallel = 1
	}
	f := &fanout{plans: plans, w: w, opts: opts, log: opts.logger(), chain: opts.chain(), sem: make(chan struct{}, opts.Parallel)}
	stop := context.AfterFunc(ctx, func() { interruptNotice(f.log, int(f.inflight.Load())) })
	defer stop()
	for _, level := range writeLevels(plans, f.chain) {
		f.level(ctx, level)
	}
}

// writeLevels groups the change plans' indexes for Execute (spec S5 "Apply
// write order"). A server's level is the most hops any planned server's
// chain puts between them, so servers nobody goes through come first and
// the deepest bastion last; A via B via C keeps C after A even when B is
// converged. With no via: one level, in plans order. Empty levels are kept
// and cost nothing.
func writeLevels(plans []ServerPlan, chain func(config.Server) []string) [][]int {
	depth := make(map[string]int)
	for _, p := range plans {
		c := chain(p.Server)
		for i, hop := range c {
			depth[hop] = max(depth[hop], len(c)-i)
		}
	}
	var levels [][]int
	for i, p := range plans {
		if p.Class != ClassChange {
			continue
		}
		d := depth[p.Server.Name]
		for len(levels) <= d {
			levels = append(levels, nil)
		}
		levels[d] = append(levels[d], i)
	}
	return levels
}

// fanout is one Execute run's shared state.
type fanout struct {
	plans    []ServerPlan
	w        Writer
	opts     ExecOptions
	log      *slog.Logger
	chain    func(config.Server) []string
	sem      chan struct{}
	hops     fleet.HopLimiter
	inflight atomic.Int32
}

// level writes one level's plans and returns when every one has finished.
func (f *fanout) level(ctx context.Context, idx []int) {
	var wg sync.WaitGroup
	for _, i := range idx {
		wg.Add(1)
		go func() {
			defer wg.Done()
			f.one(ctx, &f.plans[i])
		}()
	}
	wg.Wait()
}

// one takes the server's bastion slots — two per bastion, since the login
// probe opens a second hop connection while the write's stays open — then a
// pool slot, then writes. Both waits come before applyOne starts the
// per-server budget, and the bastion slots before the pool slot, so a queue
// behind one bastion neither burns timeouts nor idles the pool. Ctrl-C while
// waiting leaves the server pending.
func (f *fanout) one(ctx context.Context, p *ServerPlan) {
	release, err := f.hops.Acquire(ctx, f.chain(p.Server), 2)
	if err != nil {
		p.OutcomeErr = errInterrupted
		return
	}
	defer release()
	select {
	case f.sem <- struct{}{}:
	case <-ctx.Done():
		p.OutcomeErr = errInterrupted
		return
	}
	defer func() { <-f.sem }()
	if ctx.Err() != nil { // both select cases were ready
		p.OutcomeErr = errInterrupted
		return
	}
	f.inflight.Add(1)
	defer f.inflight.Add(-1)
	applyOne(ctx, p, f.w, f.opts.Held, f.opts.PerServerTimeout, f.log)
}
```

```go
// managedServers is every server adopt reads: a jump_only server is a
// tunnel that is never read, so it is not part of "read X of N" and never
// unreadable (spec S5). The emitted manifest still carries it.
func managedServers(servers []config.Server) []config.Server {
	return slices.DeleteFunc(slices.Clone(servers), func(s config.Server) bool { return s.JumpOnly })
}
```

```go
// TestApplyAdoptSweepNeverTouchJumpOnly (spec S5 Tests): apply writes the
// dependent through the bastion and lists the bastion as jump only; adopt
// reads 1 of 1; revoke --sweep prints not swept. The bastion runs no
// command in any of them: it only forwards.
func TestApplyAdoptSweepNeverTouchJumpOnly(t *testing.T) {
	t.Run("apply", func(t *testing.T) {
		hop, target, hopKey, targetKey, _, cfg := bastionFleet(t)
		kh := sshtest.KnownHostsFile(t, hop.KnownHostsLine(hopKey.PublicKey()), target.KnownHostsLine(targetKey.PublicKey()))
		var out, errBuf bytes.Buffer
		if code := run([]string{"apply", "--yes", "--config", cfg, "--known-hosts", kh}, &out, &errBuf); code != 0 {
			t.Fatalf("exit = %d, want 0\nstdout: %s\nstderr: %s", code, out.String(), errBuf.String())
		}
		if !strings.Contains(out.String(), fmt.Sprintf("bastion (jump@127.0.0.1:%d)  jump only (never read or written)", hop.Port)) ||
			!strings.Contains(out.String(), "web-1: applied · verified") {
			t.Errorf("report:\n%s", out.String())
		}
		if len(hop.Commands()) != 0 || len(hop.Forwards()) < 2 {
			t.Errorf("bastion ran %q, forwarded %v; want no command, and the read, write and probe forwarded", hop.Commands(), hop.Forwards())
		}
	})
	t.Run("adopt", func(t *testing.T) {
		hop, target, hopKey, targetKey, _, cfg := bastionFleet(t)
		kh := sshtest.KnownHostsFile(t, hop.KnownHostsLine(hopKey.PublicKey()), target.KnownHostsLine(targetKey.PublicKey()))
		var out, errBuf bytes.Buffer
		if code := run([]string{"adopt", "--config", cfg, "--known-hosts", kh}, &out, &errBuf); code != 0 {
			t.Fatalf("exit = %d, want 0\nstderr: %s", code, errBuf.String())
		}
		if !strings.Contains(errBuf.String(), "read 1 of 1 server") || !strings.Contains(out.String(), "jump_only: true") {
			t.Errorf("stderr:\n%s\nmanifest:\n%s", errBuf.String(), out.String())
		}

		alice := strings.TrimSpace(string(ssh.MarshalAuthorizedKey(sshtest.Signer(t, sshtest.Key(1)).PublicKey())))

access:
  - {user: alice, servers: ["*"]}

`, alice, bob, hop.Port, target.Port))
		kh := sshtest.KnownHostsFile(t, hop.KnownHostsLine(hopKey.PublicKey()), target.KnownHostsLine(targetKey.PublicKey()))
		var out, errBuf bytes.Buffer
		if code := run([]string{"revoke", "bob", "--sweep", "--yes", "--config", cfg, "--known-hosts", kh}, &out, &errBuf); code != 0 {
			t.Fatalf("exit = %d, want 0\nstdout: %s\nstderr: %s", code, out.String(), errBuf.String())
		}

		// and adopt exit 1, with via_failed naming the bastion.
		hop, target, _, targetKey, _, cfg := bastionFleet(t)
		kh := sshtest.KnownHostsFile(t, hop.KnownHostsLine(sshtest.Signer(t, sshtest.Key(42)).PublicKey()), target.KnownHostsLine(targetKey.PublicKey()))
		var out, errBuf bytes.Buffer
		if code := run([]string{"apply", "--dry-run", "--json", "--config", cfg, "--known-hosts", kh}, &out, &errBuf); code != 1 ||
			!strings.Contains(out.String(), `"via_failed": "bastion"`) {
			t.Errorf("apply: exit = %d, want 1 with via_failed\nstdout: %s\nstderr: %s", code, out.String(), errBuf.String())
		}
		out.Reset()
		errBuf.Reset()
		if code := run([]string{"adopt", "--config", cfg, "--known-hosts", kh}, &out, &errBuf); code != 1 {
			t.Errorf("adopt: exit = %d, want 1\nstderr: %s", code, errBuf.String())
		}
		bob := strings.TrimSpace(string(ssh.MarshalAuthorizedKey(sshtest.Signer(t, sshtest.Key(2)).PublicKey())))
		alice := strings.TrimSpace(string(ssh.MarshalAuthorizedKey(sshtest.Signer(t, sshtest.Key(1)).PublicKey())))
		withBob := writeConfig(t, fmt.Sprintf(`
users:
  - {name: alice, keys: ["%s"]}
  - {name: bob, keys: ["%s"]}
servers:
  - {name: bastion, host: 127.0.0.1, port: %d, user: jump, jump_only: true}
  - {name: web-1, host: 127.0.0.1, port: %d, user: deploy, via: bastion}
access:
  - {user: alice, servers: ["*"]}
  - {user: bob, servers: [web-1]}
`, alice, bob, hop.Port, target.Port))
		out.Reset()
		errBuf.Reset()
		if code := run([]string{"revoke", "bob", "--yes", "--config", withBob, "--known-hosts", kh}, &out, &errBuf); code != 1 {
			t.Errorf("revoke: exit = %d, want 1\nstdout: %s\nstderr: %s", code, out.String(), errBuf.String())
		}
		if len(target.Attempts()) != 0 {
			t.Error("the target was reached past a changed bastion key")
		}
	})
	t.Run("changed target key", func(t *testing.T) {
		// The target's own changed key behind a working bastion: exit 1 for
		// apply (it never pages a write command in phase 1), no via_failed.
		hop, target, hopKey, _, _, cfg := bastionFleet(t)
		kh := sshtest.KnownHostsFile(t, hop.KnownHostsLine(hopKey.PublicKey()), target.KnownHostsLine(sshtest.Signer(t, sshtest.Key(43)).PublicKey()))
		var out, errBuf bytes.Buffer
		if code := run([]string{"apply", "--dry-run", "--json", "--config", cfg, "--known-hosts", kh}, &out, &errBuf); code != 1 {
			t.Fatalf("apply: exit = %d, want 1\nstdout: %s\nstderr: %s", code, out.String(), errBuf.String())
		}
		if !strings.Contains(out.String(), `"error_kind": "host_key_changed"`) || strings.Contains(out.String(), `"via_failed"`) {
			t.Errorf("want the target's own host_key_changed and no via_failed:\n%s", out.String())
		}
	})
}
```

`bastionFleet` returns the target's HOME as its fifth value, so the sweep row can seed bob's key there.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the test.** Create `cmd/sshepherd/bastion_e2e_test.go`:

```go
//go:build integration

package main

import (
	"os"
	"path/filepath"
	"strings"
	"testing"
)

// TestE2EBastion drives the built binary through container A as a bastion
// to container C, which only A can reach (spec S5 Tests): apply and audit
// through a managed bastion; audit exit 0 through a jump_only bastion whose
// account has a nologin shell; a changed bastion key is exit 2 for audit
// (one alert, via_failed) and 1 for apply; a refused forward is 1; and the
// forwarding guard refuses a bastion key line that cannot forward.
func TestE2EBastion(t *testing.T) {
	bin, host, port, kh, pub := itE2E(t)
	target := os.Getenv("SSHEPHERD_IT_TARGET_HOST")
	if target == "" {
		t.Fatal("SSHEPHERD_IT_TARGET_HOST not set: scripts/integration.sh must start the internal container")
	}
	dir := t.TempDir()
	mk := func(name, bastionUser, bastionFlags, keyOpts, webUser string) string {
		return writeE2EManifest(t, dir, name, `
users:
  - {name: op, operator: true, keys: ['`+keyOpts+pub+`']}
servers:
  - {name: bastion, host: `+host+`, port: `+port+`, user: `+bastionUser+bastionFlags+`}
  - {name: web, host: `+target+`, port: 22, user: `+webUser+`, via: bastion}
`)
	}

	managed := mk("managed.yaml", "jump", "", "", "hope2e")
	if code, out, stderr := runBin(t, bin, "apply", "--yes", "--config", managed, "--known-hosts", kh); code != 0 {
		t.Fatalf("apply through the bastion exit = %d (stderr: %s)\n%s", code, stderr, out)
	}
	if code, out, stderr := runBin(t, bin, "audit", "--config", managed, "--known-hosts", kh); code != 0 {
		t.Fatalf("audit after apply exit = %d, want 0 (stderr: %s)\n%s", code, stderr, out)
	}

	nologin := mk("nologin.yaml", "jumpnl", ", jump_only: true", "", "hopped")
	code, out, stderr := runBin(t, bin, "audit", "--json", "--config", nologin, "--known-hosts", kh)
	if code != 0 || !strings.Contains(out, `"status": "jump_only"`) {
		t.Fatalf("audit through a nologin jump_only bastion exit = %d, want 0 (stderr: %s)\n%s", code, stderr, out)
	}

	swapped := filepath.Join(dir, "known_hosts_swapped")
	if err := os.WriteFile(swapped, []byte(swapBastionKey(t, kh, host, port)), 0o600); err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	code, out, stderr = runBin(t, bin, "audit", "--json", "--config", nologin, "--known-hosts", swapped)
	if code != 2 || !strings.Contains(out, `"via_failed": "bastion"`) || !strings.Contains(out, `"host_key_alerts": 1`) {
		t.Fatalf("audit with a changed bastion key exit = %d, want 2 with via_failed (stderr: %s)\n%s", code, stderr, out)
	}
	if code, out, stderr = runBin(t, bin, "apply", "--dry-run", "--config", managed, "--known-hosts", swapped); code != 1 {
		t.Fatalf("apply with a changed bastion key exit = %d, want 1 (stderr: %s)\n%s", code, stderr, out)
	}

	nofwd := mk("nofwd.yaml", "jumpnofwd", ", jump_only: true", "", "hopped")
	code, out, stderr = runBin(t, bin, "audit", "--json", "--config", nofwd, "--known-hosts", kh)
	if code != 1 || !strings.Contains(out, `"error_kind": "dial"`) || !strings.Contains(out, "allow TCP forwarding for this account") {
		t.Fatalf("audit through a non-forwarding bastion exit = %d, want 1 (stderr: %s)\n%s", code, stderr, out)
	}

	guarded := mk("guarded.yaml", "jump", "", "no-port-forwarding ", "hope2e")
	code, out, stderr = runBin(t, bin, "apply", "--dry-run", "--config", guarded, "--known-hosts", kh)
	if code != 1 || !strings.Contains(out, "none of the surviving keys you hold may forward through bastion") {
		t.Fatalf("apply --dry-run with a non-forwarding bastion key exit = %d, want 1 (stderr: %s)\n%s", code, stderr, out)
	}
}

// swapBastionKey returns known_hosts with the bastion's (container A's)
// keys replaced by container B's: a changed bastion key.
func swapBastionKey(t *testing.T, kh, host, port string) string {
	t.Helper()
	portB := os.Getenv("SSHEPHERD_IT_PORT_B")
	var lines []string
	for _, l := range strings.Split(strings.TrimSpace(readFile(t, kh)), "\n") {
		switch {
		case strings.HasPrefix(l, "["+host+"]:"+port+" "):
		case strings.HasPrefix(l, "["+host+"]:"+portB+" "):
			lines = append(lines, l, strings.Replace(l, "]:"+portB+" ", "]:"+port+" ", 1))
		default:
			lines = append(lines, l)
		}
	}
	return strings.Join(lines, "\n") + "\n"
}
```

**docsDelta (README, lane `writepath`).**

1. **`sshepherd apply`**: add "**Bastions.** Writes run in levels: a server before the bastions it is reached through, deepest bastion last, each level finished before the next, so a bad bastion write cannot strand its dependents' writes. A managed bastion must keep forwarding: the plan refuses a write that leaves no surviving key you hold allowed to forward to every dependent (`no-port-forwarding`, `restrict` without `port-forwarding`, or a `permitopen=` that misses one), and the post-write login check on a bastion also opens a forward to one dependent; a refused forward restores the file. A `jump_only` bastion is listed as `jump only` and never written, guarded or probed. A login check that fails at a bastion proves nothing about the server: the file is kept (`unproven`, exit 1), never restored." Copy the samples from `internal/apply/testdata/plan_jump_only.golden` and `plan_forward_refused.golden`. Document JSON `class: "jump_only"` (with `outcome: null`), `via`, `via_failed` and `summary.jump_only`. The `--parallel` row gains "at most 4 writes at a time through one bastion (each holds 2 of its 8 connections)".
2. **`sshepherd adopt`**: "`jump_only` servers are not read: they are not part of `read X of N` and are never unreadable; the generated manifest keeps them."

## Task 31: independent verifier (no lane owns this)

Check against `master` after Task 30 and report findings; change nothing.

- [ ] Schema: `via` must name another server, no loop, at most 3 bastions (exactly 3 loads); one target through two routes and two routes to one `host:port` (any user) are refused with the exact texts; the same route keeps today's "merge them into one entry". `jump_only` with `authorized_keys_file` is refused; `jump_only_unused` warns with `server` set, and validate counts the server and warns `host_key_not_recorded` for it.
- [ ] `jump_only` reach: `*`, `@tag`, groups and the operator's implicit grant skip it; `access:` cannot name it in `servers:` or `except:`; a tag only it carries is refused; `Covers`, `DesiredFor`, `GrantCount`, `serversRendering`, `wildcard_root` and `RemoveAccess` all leave it out; a tombstone naming it loads and does nothing. Grep `SelectorMatches`, `JumpOnly` and `jumpOnly` in production code and account for every site.
- [ ] Transport: `ConnectWith` is still the only place that dials; a via server with no lookup never dials directly; the hop is closed on every failure path and by `Close` (goleak green in `internal/sshconn`, `internal/sshread`, `internal/sshwrite`, `cmd/sshepherd`); Ctrl-C returns at once with a wedged tunnel; `SessionTimeout` moves the hop's deadline; the hop never inherits `Only` or `Prefer`; strict host keys on every hop (no code path accepts an unrecorded hop key).
- [ ] Errors: `via_failed` is set for a hop connect failure and a prohibited forward, and not for a target the bastion cannot reach (which carries `HopDialHint`, never `DialHint`); kinds survive; `DialHint`, `ForwardHint` and `HopDialHint` read exactly as in Task 8; an unrecorded, `@revoked` or changed-type `jump_only` bastion key stops the tunnel before any key is offered; the via host-key hint never prints a scan that runs from the operator's machine.
- [ ] Limits: `fleet.HopLimiter` is taken before the per-server budget and before the `--parallel` slot in both `audit.Run` and `apply.Execute`; 1 slot per bastion for a read, 2 for a write, root first; `fleet` stays stdlib-only (`make layering`).
- [ ] Apply: levels put dependents first and the deepest bastion last, computed from `Config.Chain` over the fleet (a converged middle hop keeps the order); `ClassJumpOnly` is never written, guarded or probed and never changes an exit; the forwarding guard and forward probe behave as Tasks 22 and 12 pin; a hop failure in the login check is UNPROVEN, never a restore and never `lockout_unrestored`.
- [ ] D28: the only new exit-2 input is a bastion host-key alarm in `audit`, counted once per bastion; `apply`, `revoke`, `rotate` and `adopt` exit 1 for it, in phase 1 and at the login check. JSON stays schema 1; every new field is omitted when empty; every pre-existing golden is byte-identical except the `DialHint` mirrors and the three help goldens.
- [ ] Import: `ProxyJump` maps per Task 17's table; a same-target or same-address host over another route is dropped without moving its grants and the output loads; `import --merge` adds no grant on a `jump_only` server, notes it once, and still compacts to `*`; `import` never sets `jump_only`.
- [ ] `adopt` reads only non-`jump_only` servers (`read X of N` excludes them) and keeps them in its output; `--sweep` prints `not swept: NAME (jump only)` with no exit change.
- [ ] Integration: the bastion tests run on alpine, debian and rocky (container A sets `AllowTcpForwarding yes`, checked by `sshd_expect`, since alpine's stock config says no); C is unreachable except through A.
- [ ] README: the `via:` section states the one-address limitation plainly (two machines at one private address behind different bastions need distinct hostnames; a future `host_key_alias:` would lift it); every sample matches its golden; README and CLAUDE.md Status agree.
- [ ] Each lane's mutation table is complete and every survivor is explained; `FuzzParseJump` has its six committed entries and `FuzzLoad` still sixteen.

## Review log

Two independent reviews of the draft (35 items). Each was checked against the code at `51e7ea0` and the design before it was applied.

- **Review 0 (19), all applied.** 1 `via_failed` golden `json_via_failed.golden.json` and mutants 19a/19b (Tasks 23, 29). 2 unrecorded, `@revoked` and changed-type `jump_only` hop rows (Task 9), plus `TestUnknownBastionKeyIsNoAlert`, exit 1 (Task 13). 3 and 4 `changed jump_only bastion key` and `changed target key` subtests (Tasks 14, 27). 5 `TestHostKeyAlertsDedupeManagedBastion` and mutant 19a (Tasks 13, 15). 6 `TestHopIgnoresTargetOnly` and mutant 9b. 7 `TestHopOfferingFallsBack` and mutant 9a. 8 the whole-chain cap tests and mutants 17a, 14a and 14b; the apply test sets `Server.Via`, or the mutant survives. 9 the two-bastions row. 10-12 `TestImportMergeRouteConflict` and `TestImportHopOnDroppedHostNotMapped` with mutants 21 and 23 (Task 20); item 12 needs no code. 13 `TestImportJSONCarriesVia` and mutant 22. 14 decodes `sweep.jump_only`, because `MarshalIndent` splits arrays over lines and a raw-text match would never hit. 15 `TestHopDeathInProbeIsExit1`, which pins P08. 16 `HopDialHint` (Tasks 8 and 9, gap 2, design amendment 3, both docsDeltas). 17 the `audit --server db-1` subtest. 18 gap 17 moves to a main-session confirmation, and the plan keeps the design's reading. 19 `TestJumpOnlyGroupWildcard`.
- **Review 1 (16), all applied.** 1 `newServer`'s signature. 2 `AllowTcpForwarding yes` with an `sshd_expect` on container A. 3 the CAS loops have bodies. 4 `dropBroken` takes snapshot verdicts in one pass; old mutant 9 is replaced, since one pass is a fixed point. 5 and 10 the expected texts are corrected. 6 the hop-death test empties the hop's `AuthorizedKeysFile` on the second connect, so it fails before Task 12. 7 the wedged-tunnel test freezes only after the target reads the client's first byte. 8 `read 2 of 2 servers`. 9 the `git diff` path filter. 11 tests that only pin behaviour are marked as such, and the `b.fleet` change moves to Task 19. 12 two `FuzzLoad` seeds. 13 the mutant count is 5. 14 two phantom file-structure entries are removed. 15 Base is `51e7ea0`. 16 `hopIndex.differs` adds two `TestMapRoutes` rows and mutants 9a/9b.
- **Follow-on.** No test uses `sshtest.ServerConfig.Accept` any more, so Task 7 drops it.
- **Rejected:** none. Nothing in either review contradicts the design. Item 0-18 is the one point that needs the main session (gap 17).

## Wave lanes (for the runner)

- **`plan`** (the runner's `args.plan`): `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-10-p10-s5-bastions.md`, committed before round 1.
- **`handoffs`** (the runner's `args.handoffs`), one entry: "P10-S5, per lane in the order schema, transport, import, writepath: git apply that lane's patch, apply its docsDelta (README and CLAUDE.md; Task 5 for schema; Task 15 for transport; Task 20 for import; Task 29 for writepath) before make check, then make check (make integration for lanes transport and writepath, all three distros for writepath), then commit patch and docsDelta together, one commit per lane. Lane writepath's TestHelpExamplesAndExitCodes reads the write commands' exit footer from README, so Task 29's README item 4 must be applied before its make check. Lane writepath's commit also carries Task 30 Steps 2-4."

Rounds run in sequence and each lane builds only on the previous rounds' commits. Round 1 holds `schema` alone (every other lane reads its fields). Round 2 holds `transport` and `import`, which own disjoint files and both need only round 1. Round 3 holds `writepath`, which needs `transport`'s `fleet.HopLimiter`, `fleet.FailedHopOf`, `audit.ServerHead` and bastion fixture accounts. Globs are shell `case` patterns (`*` crosses `/`). `README.md` and `CLAUDE.md` are integrator-only: each lane reports its `docsDelta`, and the integrator applies it after the patch and before `make check`.
